Test the kill, not only the throw: a pass that never settles once it has written its marker (a promise that never resolves), then the retry or the lease's end must run the work (`pool-retry-alarm.test.ts`, `night-cost.int.test.ts` "a pool pass cut short"), and kills in a row must wait longer each time ("5, 10, 20 minutes").

### 59. Work that runs again after a cut, writing with fresh ids
- **Bug.** Found in the class 58 sweep of fix wave 10–15, round 4: the assistant's queue read each due text, wrote its reading through `apply` (fresh `newId()` event ids), logged it, told the person, and saved what was left of the queue only after the whole loop. A drain cut short after a write (an isolate reset while it told the person in Telegram or read the next text) left the text queued, and the alarm's next drain read it again and wrote a second task.
- **Missed because.** The queue tests drained once, to the end. Nothing stopped a drain between its write and its save, and a retry looked like a first run.
- **Rule.** Work that can run again after it was cut short (an alarm the runtime retries or that fires again, a drain, a redelivered webhook) writes with ids derived from what it acts on, so a second run writes nothing new, and checks the log before it redoes the work or its outward effects (a reading that may come out otherwise, a message). The log, written with the events in one go, is the record that the work was done; a progress marker saved afterwards is only a shortcut. Test the cut: the work stops right after its write (a dependency after it fails), then runs again, and the write exists once. A derived id that replaces a monotonic one (a ULID) must keep its sort order: events at one instant are ordered by id in code units, so a numeric part is zero-padded (`deferredReadId`'s place, six digits), and a property pins it (for every i < j, `compareEvents` puts id i before id j at one `occurredAt`).

### 60. A decision taken on a snapshot that the step just before it made stale
- **Bug.** Round-5 review I-1 of fix wave 10–15 (on main since the pool's retries): `notify` delivered a notification and then asked `hasSendRetry` whether a copy had failed, passing the row `insertNotification` had answered before the delivery. Its Telegram copy read `pending` there whatever happened, since `sendToTelegram` writes `failed` to the store, not into the caller's object. A sign-in notice ("Not me") whose only copy was Telegram and whose send failed once got no retry a minute on and waited for the pool's own time, up to a day.
- **Missed because.** The retry tests had a phone with FCM failing beside Telegram: FCM's failure is read from the delivery log, so the alarm was armed anyway, and the checks asked only `getAlarm()).not.toBeNull()`, which also held because workerd's own scheduler fired those 2026 alarms at once and armed them again (class 54). Nothing checked the exact time on a store whose own time was later, with Telegram the only copy.
- **Rule.** A step that decides on what a write just changed reads it back from the store, or gets it from the write's own answer, never from an object read before the write. A helper that decides on a row's state takes the row's id (the notification), not the row, and reads the state itself (`hasSendRetry`), so a caller cannot hand it a stale copy. A retry or "nothing to do" check is tested with the failing channel alone, against an exact time on a store that already waits for a later one.

### 61. A navigator mounted after the router already wrote the address
- **Bug.** QA round 17 M2: a direct load (or F5) of Day, Inbox, More or the share target `/add` rewrote
  the address to `/`, so the next reload landed on Now. The root layout's route gate showed a blank page
  while the session and the walk-through mark were read, so the tabs navigator mounted a few frames after
  the root stack. In between, the router wrote the address of a state whose tabs route held only the
  address's unconsumed nested state, which it spells `/`; when the tabs took that state no change was
  announced, and the address stayed `/`. Stack screens (`/week`, `/task/…`) have no nested navigator and
  kept theirs.
- **Missed because.** The route sweep opened every route by a direct load but only checked that something
  rendered, not where the address ended; jest mocks the router, so no app test can see it.
- **Rule.** A navigator is mounted together with the navigators inside it: anything that waits (a session
  read, a mark from storage) holds the navigator back above it (`NavigatorHold`, once, then never again),
  never between a navigator and its nested one. The route sweep asserts that every signed-in direct load
  keeps its own address (except the screens that only redirect).
- **Rule (anything that navigates).** What navigates on its own (a notification tap, a shared link) runs
  under the hold, from inside the Stack's `layout` past the route gate, never above `NavigatorHold`; a
  cold start is tested through the real router (`expo-router/testing-library`'s `renderRouter`) with
  the hold still reading, and Back is asserted.
- **Rule (follows each tap once across remounts).** The follower clears the module's last response
  (`clearLastNotificationResponse`) and keeps the taken notification's identifier at module scope, and
  follows a link only signed in on a gated screen: the gate lets the bare paths (login, walk-through)
  through without a session, and a push from there is sent straight back. The router test's layout
  reads the real path (a constant `/` never redirects), counts path changes over a few timer turns after
  a log-out, and bounds the follower's mounts so a loop fails instead of hanging.

### 62. A copy still loading shown as the final answer
- **Bug.** QA round 17 M4: on a device with nothing local (a new browser, after logging out) every screen
  said "Nothing to do right now.", "No projects yet", … for as long as the first pull took, and in the
  browser's language before the account's.
- **Missed because.** App tests start from a filled world or an empty one, never an empty copy waiting
  for its pull; e2e pulls a few events from a local Worker in well under a second and asserts the end
  state.
- **Rule.** A screen tells "empty" from "not known yet". Screens fed by the local log are covered as a
  whole while a signed-in copy that holds nothing has not finished a sync for this session
  (`useIsFirstSync`, `FirstSyncCover`: placeholders, no words in either language, the app mounted but
  not drawn); a failed sync ends the cover and the offline line says why. Screens fed by a server call
  show their empty copy only once loaded (`status === "loaded"`). Test with the pull held: an app test
  with a deferred route, an e2e with the pull delayed that samples the page.
- **Rule (a cover has a bound).** Whatever hides the app while it waits has an upper bound and a way
  out, and every network call it waits on has a timeout: the first-sync cover gives the app back after
  `FIRST_SYNC_COVER_MS` under a "still syncing" line, and an HTTP sync call gives up after
  `HTTP_SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS`. Only a run of the current session ends the cover or claims "offline" (an
  earlier session's failure stays in `lastError`). Test with a pull that never answers, on fake timers.
- **Rule (a failure is not an answer).** A first sync that failed told nothing either: the placeholders
  (`EmptyState`, `EmptyText`, `FirstSyncBody`, `KnownAfterSync`, the task form's category, importance
  and project) all wait on one hook, `useIsAccountUnknown` (a run of this session succeeded), and the
  sync frame says "not loaded yet" with Retry for a failure that reached the server (QA round 21 M4: a
  500 drew "Nothing to do" with no word). `useIsFirstSync` only lifts the whole-app cover. Test with
  the pull failing, not only held: offline and HTTP 500.

### 63. A subscription nobody owns the end of
- **Bug.** QA round 17 review m1: the client listened to the other tabs and threw the unsubscribe away,
  and the channel was never closed, so a runtime that was created and then dropped (React's second
  effect run in development, a runtime replaced for another server) kept a full copy of the state
  alive, adopting every write of the live runtime for as long as the tab lived.
- **Missed because.** Tests build one client and let the process end; nothing dropped a client and
  checked that it stopped hearing.
- **Rule.** Whatever a factory subscribes to or opens (a listener, a channel, a socket) comes with the
  way to end it, and the owner that drops the object calls it (`PaceClient.close`, called by
  `PaceProvider` for a runtime it replaced or never used). Test it by closing and asserting that
  nothing more is heard and the resource is closed.

### 64. A failure classified around one step of an operation, not all of it
- **Bug.** QA round 17 round-3 review m-b: `reach` turned a fetch that failed into `ApiUnreachable`
  (offline), but the body is read after it: a connection cut while the answer's body was read threw
  the platform's `TypeError` past it, so the run failed with the offline line hidden (`online` stayed
  true), as if the server had answered wrongly.
- **Missed because.** The fakes fail the fetch itself or answer whole; none hands over the headers
  and then fails the body, which is how a dropped connection looks on a slow page.
- **Rule.** Where failures are sorted into kinds (unreachable, timed out, refused, broken contract),
  the sorting wraps every step that can fail that way: the request and the reading of its answer
  (`readAnswer` beside `reach`), and an abort is read the same way at each step (the sync timeout
  maps an abort in the body read to `SyncTimeout` too). Test each step failing on its own: the fetch
  rejects; the headers come and the body fails; the body stalls until the timeout; a body that is
  not JSON stays an answer.

### 65. A one-shot taken by its content, not by its arrival
- **Bug.** QA round 17 round-4 review R2: the share into Pace was "taken once" by remembering the
  text taken, and the composer was keyed by that text, so the same text shared a second time (after
  the first was added) matched what was taken: nothing remounted and the line stayed empty.
- **Missed because.** Every share test sent a different text the second time; none repeated the
  same content.
- **Rule.** Something that arrives (a share, a tap, a message) is taken once per arrival, keyed by
  what makes the arrival itself unique: the object the source hands out for each one, a counter, a
  notification's identifier. Two arrivals with equal content are two arrivals. Whatever remounts on
  arrival (a composer filled from it) is keyed by that arrival, not by the content. Test it by
  sending the same content twice, with the first one used up in between.

### 66. A fake of a platform hook that copies one of its ways in
- **Bug.** QA round 17 round-4 review R3: the notifications fake's `useLastNotificationResponse`
  read the phone's last tap on mount and heard a clear, but never listened for taps as the real hook
  does (`addNotificationResponseReceivedListener` with `determineNextResponse`). A tap while the app
  runs was invisible to every test, so nothing could show what the link follower does with a second
  tap, or with the same notification delivered again.
- **Missed because.** The fake was written for the cold-start bug in front of it; the hook's other
  ways in were not read off the module's source.
- **Rule.** A fake of a platform hook or emitter copies every way the module's own code lets state
  in (the read on mount, each listener, the clear) from the installed version's source, and names
  that version; a test covers each of them (cold, warm, again, cleared), so a fake that drops one
  shows up as a failing test, not as code nobody can exercise.

### 67. A request joined to a run that already read its input
- **Bug.** CI run 41380865441 (E1): a task written while a sync run's push hung (on a socket that
  went quiet when the browser went offline) asked for a run; the timer's `syncNow` joined the run in
  flight, whose outbox was listed before the task existed, and the next run was set a whole poll
  interval later. The offline line cleared, the task reached the server minutes after reconnect.
- **Missed because.** "Concurrent calls share one run" was tested with calls made before the run
  read anything. No test made the change after the run had listed its outbox and before it ended.
- **Rule.** Coalescing requests into a run is right only for requests made before that run reads
  its input. A request made while a run is under way gets the run after it (one, shared by every
  request made meanwhile), so the promise it holds covers everything written before it. Test it by
  holding the run after it read its input, making the change and the request, releasing, and
  asserting the change goes out at once.

### 68. A status that only something else happening can update
- **Bug.** QA round 18 M4: the offline line read the last sync run's reachability, and with the
  socket up the next run was five minutes away (30 s once it dropped). A tab left open while the
  server stopped showed no line until the person wrote something.
- **Missed because.** The offline tests turned the browser offline (an event the app hears) or
  wrote something (which runs a sync); none took the server away from an idle tab.
- **Rule.** A status shown to the person is re-checked on every signal that can change it (here the
  live socket dropping), not only on the person's next action or a slow poll; the re-check keeps the
  backoff of the thing it runs, and fires once per change of the signal, not for every retry. Test
  it with no user action at all: take the dependency away, wait for the status, bring it back, wait
  for it to clear, and count the requests meanwhile.

### 69. A reason inferred from something found missing in shared state
- **Bug.** Fix round 5 V1 (and CI run 41400987398): the sign-in card said "You signed out in another
  tab." whenever a 401 found the browser's shared session store empty. Other things empty it: a
  second tab hearing the same revoke from another device a moment earlier, storage the browser
  blocks (always empty), and the log-out itself, whose tab cleared the store only once the server
  answered (the first tab's 401 came before that and said "another device" in CI).
- **Missed because.** The one test cleared the shared store by hand, which reads the same in all
  those cases; no test had two tabs hear one revoke, a store that keeps nothing, or a log-out whose
  answer was still on its way.
- **Rule.** A reason told to the person (who ended it, where, why) comes from a positive signal the
  actor leaves *before* it acts (here a note with a mark of the token, written by the tab that logs
  out before its request goes), never from shared state found empty or changed, which many actors
  and failures produce. With no signal, say the default that is true in every case. Test it with
  two actors sharing the state: the other cause first, a store that keeps nothing or fails, and the
  real cause with its effect held mid-way.

### 70. A backoff reset by a step that does not prove the trouble is over
- **Bug.** Fix round 5 V2: the live socket reset its reconnect backoff on every `open`, and every
  drop of a connected socket asked for a sync run. A link that lets a socket in and cuts it 100 ms
  later reconnected every second and pulled over HTTP every second: 60 opens and 60 pulls a minute
  (30 before round 4 added the run on a drop).
- **Missed because.** The backoff tests made reconnects fail before they opened, and the drop tests
  dropped sockets that had opened; none had a socket that opens and dies at once, again and again.
- **Rule.** A retry's backoff (and anything else that fires "once per recovery") is reset only by
  evidence that the trouble is over: a connection that stayed up a while, an answer to a real
  request, not the first half of a handshake. Test it with a flapping dependency over a long fake
  minute, counting attempts and the work each one triggers.

### 71. A browser taken for a computer with a keyboard
- **Bug.** Fix round 5 V4: QA round 18 M1 put every web sheet's focus on its first text field, so
  on a phone's browser or an installed PWA the on-screen keyboard came up over Edit task and the
  pickers as they opened. Round 4 had already hidden the keyboard hints on touch (QA 18 L5), but
  the focus was written for "the web" rather than for "a keyboard".
- **Missed because.** The focus e2e ran at phone width in desktop Chrome (a fine pointer), which is
  a narrow computer, not a phone; the touch e2e only looked at hints.
- **Rule.** Anything done for a keyboard (shortcuts shown, a text field focused for the person, a
  hint to press a key) is gated on the one keyboard check (`KEYBOARD` / `IS_TOUCH_BROWSER`, the fine
  pointer), never on "web" or "wide". The touch e2e (`touch-hints.e2e.ts`, an emulated phone and a
  wide touch screen) opens every sheet with a field and asserts no text field takes focus.

### 72. A way in that only an untold gesture opens
- **Bug.** QA round 19 M2: finishing a task at another time, or cancelling or skipping it with a
  time, was reachable only by a one-second press on Done; the sentence that said so was an
  accessibility hint nobody sees, and the sheet's "Close task as… Cancelled · Skipped" was a grey
  caption that did not read as a control.
- **Missed because.** The app test fired `longPress` on Done itself, so the way in existed for the
  test; nothing asked whether a person could find it.
- **Rule.** Every way into a screen or sheet has a visible control (a button, a link, a menu item)
  on every platform; a gesture (long press, swipe, double click) is a shortcut to it, never the
  only door, and what it does is in a tooltip on the control. Controls look like controls (a
  button, a chip), not captions. Test the visible way in, not the gesture.

### 73. A decorative color checked at rest but shown in motion, on a token no test measures
- **Bug.** QA round 19 L2: the loading placeholder's rows used `track` (a 12 % wash meant for a
  progress bar's groove) and breathed down to 45 % opacity, so in dark they were about 1.1:1 on the
  page: a black screen with a faint line of text.
- **Missed because.** The contrast tests cover text, marks and outlines; a placeholder is none of
  them, and its color was borrowed from another role and then faded by an animation the tests do
  not see.
- **Rule.** Every color with a job on screen has its own token with that job's contrast floor in
  `contrast.test.ts`, measured where it is weakest (the low point of an animation, the lighter of
  the backgrounds it sits on). Placeholders: 1.5:1 at the dim of their breath (`PLACEHOLDER_DIM`).

### 74. A stale actor's clean-up of state it shares with newer ones
- **Bug.** Round 6 review I1: a hidden tab still holding an ended session (T1) got its 401 after
  the person had logged out in another tab and signed in again there (T2). Its sign-out cleared
  the browser's shared session store (T2 gone: the other tab was signed out on its next reload and
  T2 stayed in Devices) and wiped the shared IndexedDB copy, the other tab's unsent outbox included.
- **Missed because.** Every sign-out test had one tab, or two tabs on the same session; none let a
  second actor replace the shared state before the first actor's late clean-up ran.
- **Rule.** Clean-up of shared state is compare-and-clear: an actor clears only what is still its
  own (the store still holds the token it signs out), and work tied to a shared resource (wiping
  the local copy) runs only when nobody else holds it (no session left in the store). A different
  owner taking over is handled by the owner, by identity (a new store id drops the old copy on its
  first pull), never by a stale actor. Test it with two actors: the second replaces the state, then
  the first's late event arrives; the second's state and data are intact.

### 75. A request sent in the gap of a hand-over
- **Bug.** e2e flake of `sessions.e2e.ts` "ending this device signs out…" (3/60 on the base, 13/120
  here): ending this browser's own session stopped the socket but not the sync, so the first
  sync's pull or push (and the session check its 401 set off) went out with the token after the
  server had ended it, and answered 401.
- **Missed because.** The app test ended the session with an idle sync; the e2e started right after
  sign-in, when the first sync is still running, but only sometimes long enough to overlap.
- **Rule.** Before an action that ends the credential everything uses, every sender is quiesced and
  kept quiet until the credential is forgotten: loops stopped, the run in flight finished
  (`sync.hold()`), the socket rested, and starters that would begin again (the signed-in
  bootstrap) check the hand-over (`auth.isEnding()`). Test it by asking the sender at the moment of
  the ending request (the DELETE route calls `syncNow()` and expects `stopped`) and by looping the
  e2e that counts every request answered after it.
- **Rule (the check after a 401).** The same gap opens when the server ends the session and the
  client only suspects it: a 401 for the session in use (or the socket's session-ended close) sets
  off the `me` check, and until it answers, every other sender still holds the token. CI run
  41668309388: a tab still starting had its first pull refused, the bootstrap went on to read the
  bell (`/api/notifications`) while `me` was on its way, and that read was refused after the tab
  showed the login card. So a check under way holds every sender that would carry the session:
  `createApiClient`'s `sessionCheck` makes a call (but `me` and the log-out) wait for the answer and
  then send whatever is current (nothing, once it ended); the live channel's `sessionCheck` makes a
  (re)connect wait the same way. Both senders read one `sessionChecks` in `create-client.ts`, and the
  socket's close joins the same check. Test it with the check held: a held `me` and a call or a
  socket started meanwhile (`create-client.test.ts`, `live.test.ts`), and the e2e that holds a
  starting tab's first pull until the end and slows `me` (`live.e2e.ts`, "a tab still starting…").

### 76. A guard that cannot tell the case it is for from the one it must leave alone
- **Bug.** Round 6 review M4: the sheet's dim refused every focus (blurred to the page) to keep the
  modal's focus trap from landing on it; a screen reader moving onto the dim from the sheet was
  thrown to the page too.
- **Missed because.** The guard was tested only with the case it was written for (Tab and Shift+Tab
  in the e2e); nothing moved focus onto the dim the way a reader does.
- **Rule.** A guard keys on what identifies its case (here where the focus came from: the trap
  gives it from outside the modal, its opener or a focus bracket; a reader from inside the sheet),
  not on the effect both cases share. Test both: the case it refuses and the one it must let
  through (`refuse-focus.web.test.ts`, the e2e focuses the dim from the sheet and it stays).

### 77. A hand-off between two screens tested with both mounted at once
- **Bug.** QA round 20 M3 (and round 6 review M3): a share arriving in the page replaced Now's
  half-typed line, and a blank one wiped it. Now's line noted its text for the share target and
  took the note back when it unmounted; a route change unmounts "/" before the share target reads
  the note, so the share was handed over alone (a blank one as an empty line), and a share that
  opens a new page of the tab found no note at all.
- **Missed because.** The unit test mounted the share target and Now side by side and called the
  note function by hand, so the line never left before the read; nothing went through a route
  change or a new page.
- **Rule.** What one screen hands to the next outlives the screen that leaves: it is kept where the
  next one will read it (here the tab's sessionStorage), and a screen's clean-up never clears it on
  the way out; it is cleared by what makes it moot (the line emptied). Test a hand-off the way it
  happens: the first screen typed into and unmounted, then the second mounted (and a fresh page
  that has only the storage); the e2e types, then navigates in the page and by a new page.

### 78. A summary that drops what tells the cases apart
- **Bug.** QA round 20 L8 and L5: History named a cancelled and a skipped task "Task closed", like a
  done one, right after the close sheet had asked which it was; the close sheet's preview said
  "happened 18:00" for yesterday evening and for Oct 31 alike.
- **Missed because.** The label and the preview were tested with the common case (a done task, a
  time today), where the dropped part has one value and nothing is lost.
- **Rule.** A label or summary keeps every part that the person chose or that differs between the
  cases it stands for (an outcome, a day that is not today); it is built from the record, not from
  its type alone. Test each case the summary must tell apart, not only the first.

### 79. Two callers of one read, each fetching it
- **Bug.** QA round 20 L9: the first load sent two identical pulls of the log (the signed-in start's
  run, then the loop's first tick at once), two of the notification pool (the start, then the
  socket's first connection "back on the line") and two of today's calendar (the time bar's button
  and the calendar prompt).
- **Missed because.** Each caller was right on its own and tested on its own; nothing counted the
  requests of a whole first load.
- **Rule.** A read with several callers that come together shares one request in flight (keyed by
  what it asks), and a follow-up that exists to catch up ("the loop's first tick", "back on the
  line") skips when the same thing was just read and nothing was written since. Test it by counting
  a whole start's requests (the e2e first-load test: no URL twice, one pull since 0).

### 80. A field that reads half of a value and guesses the rest, with no way to say it
- **Bug.** Round 6 (the owner, after QA rounds 18–19): a block left running Fri 20:00 → Sun 07:00
  could not be cut back to Friday from the Day sheet. Its To field reads a clock time only; the day
  was guessed near the old end (rules (a)–(c), at most 12 hours either way), so Friday 23:00 was
  refused ("The end must be after the start.") and every other time landed on Saturday or Sunday.
- **Missed because.** Every test typed a clock and asked which day the rules chose; the tests were
  about guessing well, and none asked whether a person could say the day when the guess is wrong.
- **Rule.** A value typed in parts (a day and a clock, an amount and a unit) has a control for
  every part the person may need to change; a guess fills a part only while the person has not
  said it, and once said it is read as said, refused in the usual words if it cannot be. Test the
  answer the guess never gives (Friday for a weekend block), not only the guesses.

### 81. A key the system needs asked of the person
- **Bug.** QA round 18 L4: New preset showed a bare "Id" field next to Name (filled from the name
  in Latin letters only, so a Russian name left it empty and Save failed with "bad id"), and the
  form opened with nothing focused.
- **Missed because.** The form test typed an English name and read the id back; it asserted the
  field existed. Nobody typed a Russian name, and nobody asked why a person should see it.
- **Rule.** Ids, slugs and keys are made by the system (`freePresetId`: from the name, Russian
  spelt in Latin letters, numbered past a taken or reserved one) and never shown as a field; a form
  for a new thing puts the cursor in its first field where there is a keyboard (class 71). Test
  with a name in the owner's language and a second thing of the same name.
- **Also: a proof the person already gave.** The same mistake for identity: the MCP consent page
  asked a browser that held the person's Pace session to sign in with Telegram again (owner bug
  2026-11-03; testing half and sweep in class 119). Before asking a person to prove who they are,
  ask whether they already have.

### 82. A name taken from the kind of screen where the thing it shows is known
- **Bug.** QA round 18 L8: every `/task/<id>` tab was "Task · Pace", every project "Projects ·
  Pace", the new preset form "Presets · Pace": a browser history and the PWA switcher listed eight
  identical rows.
- **Missed because.** The title was a pure function of the route's first segment, and its test
  checked one title per route kind.
- **Rule.** A label for one thing (a tab title, a notification, a share sheet, a back button's
  name) says that thing in the person's own words, never translated (`subjectName`), and falls
  back to the kind only while it cannot be found. Test it with a thing whose name is a word of the
  catalog ("Settings") and one in another script.

### 83. An address a server says it has handed out over the one the app has reached it at
- **Bug.** QA round 18 L10: "Connect an AI app" printed `http://localhost:8787/mcp` and "Add a
  computer" put `--api http://localhost:8787` in its command, while the app talked to the Worker
  on :8818; the Worker's `API_ORIGIN` named a port it was not running on, so both were dead.
- **Missed because.** The tests served `/api/instance` with the same origin the fake runtime used,
  so the two never differed.
- **Rule.** An address the app gives a person to use elsewhere is one the app has proven (the
  origin it talks to), not one a server reports about itself; a reported one is only a fallback
  for what the app cannot know (its web app). Test it with the two different.
- **Rule corrected (round 7 review 1, class 102).** "Proven" is wrong for an address that is also
  an identity: the MCP URL is the server's OAuth resource (`<API_ORIGIN>/mcp`), and an MCP client
  refuses tokens for any other, so it is the server's own public API (`instance.apiOrigin`), with a
  line saying it differs from the address in use; only a bearer-token address (the computer
  agent's `--api`) keeps the one the app has reached.

### 84. A cost of using the screen that no test counts: tab stops, target size, a jump
- **Bug.** QA round 19 L5: the date picker had a tab stop per day (30+ before "Next month") and
  opened on "Previous month". QA round 18 L6: the composer's icons were 36×36, the task's link row
  15 px high, narrow Week blocks 31 px wide and switches 24 px high at 390. QA round 18 L11: the
  offline line pushed the page down 37 px mid-typing.
- **Missed because.** The tests ask whether a control exists, has a name and works; none counts
  what it costs to reach it (Tab presses, the size of the thing a thumb aims at) or whether the
  page moves under the person when a status comes and goes.
- **Rule.** A grid of like choices is one tab stop with arrow keys (roving tab index) and opens on
  the chosen one; anything tapped is at least 44×44 at phone width (`hitSlop` where the drawing
  stays small); a status that comes and goes takes no room from the page (an overlay or a row kept
  for it). Guards: the e2e measures every visible control's hit box at 390, counts the date
  picker's tab stops, and measures the main heading's position across going offline.

### 85. An outside checker holds an answer to the letter of our schema, and its refusal reads as an outage
- **Bug.** The LLM regression run of 2026-11-02 (`bun test:llm`): slot 3's endpoint answered "Generated
  JSON does not match the expected schema … missing properties: 'outcome', 'label', 'start', 'end',
  'expectMinutes'". Our schema already lists every key as required and nullable (strict mode takes
  it); the endpoint runs that model best-effort, checks the answer itself and refuses it, and its
  model had left out the activity keys of a task's action, which mean "does not apply". The refusal
  was counted as a failed provider (`llm/unavailable`), and our own check would have refused the same
  answer for the same missing keys.
- **Missed because.** Every fake answer is built with `blankModelAction`, so every key is always
  there; no fake ever played an endpoint that checks the answer itself, and the refusal arrives as a
  stream error chunk with no status, which the code sorted with the outages.
- **Rule.** An answer is read as leniently as its meaning allows, by one check for every way it
  arrives (`answerCheck` in `answer-check.ts`): nullable keys an action left out, which mean "does
  not apply", are filled with null (`fillAbsent`; never a list, class 95) before the strict check. An outside checker's refusal that carries the
  payload (`failed_generation`, or what was streamed) goes through our own check before it counts as
  a failure, and when it fails ours too it is an answer out of shape, never a provider down. What we
  send is pinned against the strictest mode we may meet (every object's properties all `required`,
  `additionalProperties: false`). Test doubles copy the real endpoint's refusal, body and chunk.

### 100. A refusal the platform reports as a plain failure, retried as an outage
- **Bug.** CI run 41463968451 (round 6 M6's e2e): the session was ended while the visible tab's
  socket was still connecting; the Worker refused the upgrade with HTTP 401, which a browser gives
  the page as close code 1006, the same as a dropped network. The channel backed off and retried
  (refused each time) and only its 30 s poll could find the 401, so the tab stayed on Settings past
  the test's 15 s. Locally the socket was up before the end and heard the 4401 close.
- **Missed because.** The tests closed a socket that had opened (4401, or a drop after it lived);
  none closed one that never opened, and the e2e's order (socket up first) held on a fast machine.
- **Rule.** Where the platform hides why something was refused (a WebSocket upgrade, a CORS error,
  an opaque response), the first failure of a streak asks a request that can say it (a run, whose
  401 checks the session, whose network failure shows the offline line) instead of retrying as an
  outage; once per streak, so a dead server is not hammered (`onFailedToOpen`). Test the refusal
  before the first success, not only after it.

### 101. A helper shared by two callers, changed for one in another branch than the other's
- **Bug.** CI run 41467537358: the date picker's Shift+Tab came back to today, not the moved day.
  Round 6 M4 changed `refuseFocus` (the sheet dim's) to keep focus coming from inside the modal;
  round 6-B, in a parallel branch, had the picker's other days use it to refuse the sheet trap's
  walk. The trap walks back from the last control after focusing the first one, so every focus it
  tries comes from inside the modal: merged, the selected day kept it. Not the clock: it failed on
  any date (checked at Oct 14 and Nov 2).
- **Missed because.** Each branch was green alone; the merge commit was pushed and its first full
  e2e run was CI's. The helper's rule named its own case ("from outside the modal"), which was
  true for the dim (the first control) and false for a control in the middle.
- **Rule.** A helper keyed on what identifies one caller's case is not reused for another case;
  the picker has its own (`enterAtStop`: focus from outside the grid goes on to the stop). After
  merging parallel branches, run the full e2e on the merge before pushing it.

### 102. An address bound to an identity handed out as a plain location
- **Bug.** Round 7 review 1: Connect an AI app handed out `<reached origin>/mcp`, but the Worker's
  protected resource is `<API_ORIGIN>/mcp`; a device reaching the server at another address (a LAN
  IP, a proxy) gave MCP clients a URL whose resource they would reject.
- **Missed because.** Class 83's rule ("the address the app has proven") was applied to every
  address; the e2e reaches the Worker at its `API_ORIGIN`, so the two never differed there.
- **Rule.** Tell an address that only locates (bearer tokens work at any) from one that names
  (an OAuth resource, an issuer, a cookie's domain): the second is the server's own, said as it is.
  Test across both sides: reached at one address, the URL handed out equals the resource the
  server's metadata names (`oauth.int.test.ts`, `server-store.test.ts`).

### 103. A person's unsent words kept by the device across a change of person
- **Bug.** Round 7 review 4: the half-typed line (`pace.lineDraft`, the tab's sessionStorage)
  outlived a sign-out; signed in as someone else, a share landed after the first person's text.
- **Missed because.** The draft's tests asked whether it survives what it must (a route change, a
  new page); none signed out between typing and sharing.
- **Rule.** State kept for a person (drafts, caches, last choices) is forgotten when the person
  changes (signed-in → signed-out, beside the local copy's drop) or keyed by the session. Test:
  type, sign out, sign in as another, then the action that reads it.

### 104. A request shared in flight with no bound and no owner in its key
- **Bug.** Round 7 review 5: today's calendar read shares one request in flight by its range;
  one that never settled was joined by every later read for good, and a read after signing in as
  someone else could join the first person's request.
- **Missed because.** The sharing test (class 79) had the request answer; nothing let it stall or
  changed the session while it was out.
- **Rule.** A shared in-flight request has a deadline (`withinDeadline`) and a key with everything
  that changes its answer (the session too). Test a first call that never settles, on fake timers,
  and a second call from another session.
### 90. A guard that names by hand the screens or sheets it covers
- **Bug.** The first-sync guard listed its routes by hand: QA round 18 M5 fixed Now, round 19 M1
  found Day and the Assistant log printing zeros, round 20 M1 found Settings and Presets drawing
  defaults (and live switches that could write them over the account); each fix covered the screens
  the last round named. The sheet-focus guard listed its sheets by hand too, and the first sweep
  over a registry of every sheet found the one it lacked: a block's details on a phone's Week took
  no focus at all (nothing in it to focus but the dim), so the keyboard stayed on the page behind
  the dialog.
- **Missed because.** A hand list covers the cases someone remembered when it was written; a new
  screen or sheet, or one nobody connected to the rule, is never added, and the guard stays green.
- **Rule.** A guard for an app-wide rule (first sync, focus, Escape, tooltips, the mechanical
  matrix) loops over a list read from the source: the route files under `apps/app/app`
  (`e2e/support/routes.ts`), the sheet registry (`apps/app/src/ui/sheet-names.ts`, which `Sheet`
  requires a name from, a jest test keeping it equal to the sources, and the e2e failing on a
  registered sheet it has no opener for). Where the loop cannot reach a case, it names it with the
  reason (`NOT_ON_THE_WEB`, `BY_DESIGN`), never skips it silently. The rule's expectation must hold
  for the instance with the least in it (a sheet with no field and no button).

### 95. A lenient reader that fills the part carrying the meaning
- **Bug.** Review of round 6-C (I3): `fillAbsent` filled every required list it found missing, at
  any depth, the answer's own `actions` and `questions` included, so a model answer with no
  `actions` key passed the check as "nothing to do" and won the race; the next provider, which
  would have read the message, was cancelled.
- **Missed because.** The class 85 tests left out only the keys that mean "does not apply" (an
  action's activity fields); nothing left out a key whose absence means "no answer".
- **Rule.** A lenient reading fills only parts whose empty value means the same as their absence
  (a nullable field of an action: null), never a part that carries the answer (the root's lists,
  an action's lists). Test each kind of key left out: the one it may fill (kept), the one it may
  not (refused, and the next provider answers).

### 96. A state whose only way out is a verdict the surrounding mechanism can cancel
- **Bug.** Review of round 6-C (C1): a provider back from its penalty was probed (asked with the
  next one at once) until a read judged it. A healthy provider slower than its partner never was:
  the partner answered first, the race cancelled the probe before its first token, and a cancelled
  probe had no verdict. It stayed a probe for good, so every read asked two providers and the
  configured order flipped to the partner.
- **Missed because.** The probe tests used a probe that answered first or failed; none was healthy
  and merely slower, the common case once an outage is over.
- **Rule.** A state with an exit condition (probation, a pending retry, a claim) also leaves when
  the condition can no longer be observed: here a first token counts as recovery, and a probe the
  read could not judge goes back to its place after one try (`verdictOf`: `unjudged`). For every
  such state, test the run in which the mechanism around it (a race, a deadline, a cancel) ends
  before the exit is seen, and assert the next run is back to normal.

### 97. A record many writers change, kept where a read-modify-write can lose a change
- **Bug.** Review of round 6-C (I2, M2, M3): the providers' health, changed by every user's read
  in every isolate, was one Workers KV value read, changed and written back. KV is eventually
  consistent and has no compare-and-set: two isolates penalising two providers at once kept one
  penalty; an edge cache could hand back a cleared record for up to a minute; the writes shared
  the OAuth namespace's daily quota; a first slow answer was written too.
- **Missed because.** The store's tests ran one writer at a time against a map standing in for
  KV, which is consistent and atomic; "reads afresh before it writes" passed in sequence.
- **Rule.** State with many writers lives with one writer (a Durable Object: `LlmHealth`, its
  records applied one after another), and is written only when what outlives the writer changes;
  what only steers the next few reads stays in memory. Readers may cache it briefly but never
  cache a change the writer did not confirm, and a writer that cannot be reached is logged as an
  error while the read goes on with the default. Test with two writers at once against the real
  object, and count storage writes over a long simulated day.

### 98. An outside service's words copied into a log or a shared record, the person's words with them
- **Bug.** Review of round 6-C (M5): a failed call was logged with the start of the endpoint's
  body, and a schema refusal's body quotes the model's whole answer (`failed_generation`), which
  copies the person's message; the health memory every user shares stored the first 80
  characters of an error's message as the penalty's `reason`.
- **Missed because.** The log line and the reason were written for diagnosis and tested with
  error bodies that say nothing about the person ("Service Unavailable").
- **Rule.** What an outside service says goes into a log or a shared record only as its status,
  its code and type, and its message with any field that echoes our request removed
  (`withoutGenerations`); a shared record stores a category from a closed list (`HealthReason`),
  never free text. Test the log line with a body that quotes the person's words and assert they
  are absent.

### 99. A failure's reason dropped at a boundary, so the remedy offered is one that cannot work
- **Bug.** Review of round 6-C (I4): the parse answer said only `unavailable` and a `retryAt`. A
  read whose every model answered out of shape, and one whose models were all down with no time
  said, both came back with `retryAt: null`: the app read both as "out of requests for now" and
  offered "Read it when it's back", which the server refused to queue without a time, so each
  press asked the models again to the same end. The bot said "AI is unavailable" for an answer
  it simply could not read.
- **Missed because.** The server knew the reason (`llm/invalid-output` against `llm/unavailable`)
  and its tests checked it; the answer the client reads had no field for it, and the client's
  tests drew each status with a time, never the case with none.
- **Rule.** When a failure has kinds that call for different remedies, the reason crosses every
  boundary to the screen as a closed value read tolerantly (`reason`, `knownOrNull`), with one
  rule for what may be offered (`canReadLater`, `unreadOf`), and a remedy is shown only where the
  other side will act on it; after it is used, the screen settles (offered once, the network
  quiet). Test each reason end to end: the server's answer, the bot's words, the app's words,
  and the count of requests after the remedy is pressed.

### 105. A retried check whose failure costs more than the time it is given
- **Bug.** `time-bar.test.tsx` "keeps the picker as it was while a pick is written" failed on every
  run from one evening on (round 7 merge). The picker closed within 50 ms of the release; the
  test's `waitFor(() => expect(listsNamed("Rest")).toEqual([]))` checked once before React had
  rendered the close, and that failing `toEqual` printed a diff of the whole rendered list (its
  elements' class instances, 640 React deprecation warnings), about 1.1 s. `waitFor` gives 1 s, so
  it ran out before the retry that would have seen the picker gone. It looked tied to the clock
  (it passed earlier in the day): what moved was how long the print took on a busier or slower
  machine, around the 1 s line; no clock was read (with the clock faked to 08:00 or 21:00 the old
  check failed alike, and the new one passes at both).
- **Missed because.** A check inside `waitFor` is expected to fail a few times; nothing measured
  what one failure costs, and on a quick machine the first retry still came in time.
- **Rule.** What a retried check asserts is cheap to fail: rendered elements are compared by count
  (`toHaveLength`), by their labels, or by identity (`toBe`), never with `toEqual`. Guard: a lint
  rule (`ELEMENT_DEEP_EQUAL` in `eslint.config.ts`) refuses `toEqual`/`toStrictEqual` over
  `getAll*`/`queryAll*`/`findAll*` and the time bar's list helpers in app tests.

### 106. An element made focusable for one case, left focusable for every other
- **Bug.** Round 7 merge (CI on the merge of round 6-D): the date picker's Shift+Tab, back from the
  control after the days, landed on the sheet's own surface instead of the day moved to. 6-D had
  given every sheet's surface `tabIndex = -1` so that a sheet with nothing else to focus could
  start on it; react-native-web's modal trap, when Tab leaves the sheet, tries each element before
  its descendants, so the surface (the ancestor of everything in the sheet) took every walk, in
  both directions. 7-A's `enterAtStop` was never reached.
- **Missed because.** 6-D's unit test asked only where a sheet starts, and its e2e opened each
  sheet without leaving it by Tab; the picker's e2e ran on the other branch (class 101).
- **Rule.** An element is made focusable (even by script only) for exactly the case that needs
  it, and stops being so in every other: the surface takes `tabindex="-1"` only while it is the
  sheet's start itself (`didFocusSheetStart`). Anything focusable that wraps other controls is a
  candidate for every focus walk that tries ancestors first. Test both cases (nothing else: the
  surface; a control: the surface out of the walk), and keep the e2e that leaves the picker by Tab
  and comes back.

### 107. A quiet window opened at a step before the start's last one
- **Bug.** Round 7 merge's full e2e: `sync-state.e2e.ts` "a server gone while the tab sits idle"
  saw `POST /api/sync/push` and `GET /api/sync/pull?since=0` in its 6 s idle window. They were
  the signed-in start's last step: a new account has no time zone, the start writes the device's,
  and the loop's first tick pushes it and pulls after it, over the socket once it is open, else
  over HTTP. The test opened its window once the socket was asked for and any pull was seen; a
  socket slow to open under a loaded run let the tick go by HTTP inside the window (reproduced by
  holding the socket 1.5 s).
- **Missed because.** On a quiet machine the socket is open before the tick, so the push rides it
  as a frame, which the idle check (HTTP calls only) does not count; the window's start was a
  proxy (a pull seen) for the real end of the start.
- **Rule.** A window that asserts "nothing more is sent" opens after the documented last step of
  what came before, observed on every channel that may carry it (`hasPulledAfterPush` over
  `SyncTraffic.steps`, which records pushes and pulls by HTTP and by socket in order), never at
  an earlier step that usually comes last.
- **Rule (a window against a loop, review I4 of the PR #48 follow-ups).** A window that must see a
  loop repeat lasts at least twice its longest period: a loop of period P sends twice in a window W
  whatever its phase only when W ≥ 2P. The route sweep's long window is 11 s for loops of up to 5 s.
### 108. Responsiveness assumed, never measured: an action's visible result waits for its write
- **Bug.** The owner, 2026-11-01: on the phone, taps feel late. Measured on the web build at 4× CPU throttle (a mid-range phone): 140–360 ms from tap to the next frame for a tab switch, a filter chip, a check-off, opening a row and Back (Event Timing API). Causes: (1) `persistLocal` in `packages/client/src/state.ts:213-216` awaits the local write (SQLite on Android, IndexedDB on the web) **before** it updates the store, so every action's result waits for a disk transaction; (2) press feedback is drawn by a React re-render (NativeWind `active:` classes), Android has no native ripple and 21 of 63 pressables show nothing, and react-native-web delays the pressed state 50 ms by default (`DEFAULT_PRESS_DELAY_MS`); (3) check-off mounts its dust views in the same commit as the check fill (a 229 ms long task).
- **Missed because.**
  - The requirement "opening and checking off are instant, no spinners" (requirements › Нефункциональные) never became a number, so no test could fail.
  - Client tests run on memory adapters whose `append` resolves in a microtask, and jest-expo mocks storage, so "await the write, then show" looks instant in every test.
  - e2e and every QA round run headless Chromium on a fast server CPU with a mouse: the same 140–360 ms is 35–90 ms there, below what anyone notices. No run throttled the CPU or used touch.
  - Android QA checked that flows work, not how they feel; the release build was never timed on a mid-range phone.
  - "Local-first, so it is instant" was taken as given: local data still costs a transaction, and library defaults (react-native-web's press delay, NativeWind's JS-driven `active:`) were never audited.
- **Rule.** Every user action shows its result in the first frame from in-memory state; persistence follows, and a failed write rolls the state back and answers the action's `Result` error. Press feedback never waits for JavaScript: Android ripple (native), CSS `:active` on the web, `delayPressIn={0}`. Responsiveness has a budget: tap → next frame ≤ 100 ms (p75) at 4× CPU throttle, asserted by an e2e test; QA taps through the main flows with the CPU throttled and touch on, and the release check includes a tap-feel pass on a mid-range Android phone.
- **Task 2.5 (2026-11-02).** The store now integrates first and writes after (`packages/client/src/local-log.ts`): the next action integrates while the previous write is on its way, writes stay in order, a refused write takes back only its own events and rebuilds the state through the materializer from the rest (a later action and the server's events stay), the action answers `persist_failed` ("Could not save this on the device…"), and the sync only ever sends what is on the disk (the outbox is the store's, `onLocal` fires after the write). Measuring found what the brief had not: none of the four measured taps (tab, chip, row, check) writes on the tap itself; their time is the render they start. One more cause of that kind was in reach and fixed: the composer line gave react-native-web a new `onContentSizeChange` on every render, and react-native-web then reads the textarea's `scrollHeight` (a forced layout) on every re-render of Now, the top JavaScript cost of the row and tab taps. **Rule (added).** A handler that a library turns into a measurement (react-native-web's `onContentSizeChange` on a multiline `TextInput`, a callback ref) is one function for the component's life (`useCallback`); a re-render must not measure. Phone preferences follow the same order as events: shown, then saved, rolled back on refusal (`shownThenSaved` in `apps/app/src/platform/shown-then-saved.ts`).
- **Task 2.5 fix round 1 (review).** (a) *A wipe beside the queue.* Sign-out, a server switch (`resetLocalData`) and a reset server store (`createReset` in the sync) called `eventStore.clear()` directly, beside the new write queue: a write queued behind one in flight landed after the clear, in the wiped store, stayed in the outbox and was pushed to the next account or server. The tests held one write at a time and never wiped with a second one waiting. **Rule.** Once writes are queued, every operation that touches the same store goes through that queue: the log's `clear()` empties the state at once, clears the store in the queue after the write in flight, and a write handed over before the clear is never made (it answers `persist_failed`). Guard: held-store tests (state and sync) that hold A, stage B, wipe, and find the store, the outbox and the next push empty. Swept: every `eventStore.clear()`/`append`/`markSynced` caller outside the log (only the sync's cursor, store id and version writes remain, which hold no events). (b) *A dependent action after a refused one stays, as an orphan, by decision.* When A (say a task's creation) is refused by the disk and B (its close) was staged on top while A was in flight, B is kept and written. Undoing B too would need the client to know which events depend on which, a second copy of the materializer's rules (class 1). An orphan is harmless: the server accepts it by schema, every materializer ignores an event about an entity it does not know, nothing retries it, so the network goes quiet; the person saw "Could not save this on the device…" for A. (c) Phone answers (a dismissed calendar event, a series rule) refused by the keystore stay for the session (`usePhoneAnswers`; the server's own answer covers the next start) and never escape as an unhandled rejection, and a phone read that started before a newer one never overwrites it (`useLatestLoad`). A refused push-method save says so ("The phone could not save this choice."). A refused save restores the old value only if no later pick replaced it (`shownThenSaved`).
- **Task 2.5 fix round 2 (review).** The session overlay of phone answers (round 1) held every answer for good and was laid over each read: give "skip" or "attended" for a series on Day, remove the rule in Settings → Permissions, come back (Day stays mounted in the tabs), and the rule still held, hiding the series or logging its events by itself. The tests checked the answer while its write was pending and when refused, never after it landed and something else changed it. **Rule.** An optimistic overlay holds an answer only while its write is on its way; once it landed and a read holds it, the read wins. Only a refused write stays for the session, and it yields to any change of the phone's memory made elsewhere. Guard: Day tests that give a rule here and remove it with `removeSeriesRule` (the series is asked about again; nothing more is logged by itself), and a Now test where the phone forgets a landed dismissal and the card comes back. `useLatestLoad` keeps the shown value when a read fails.
- **Review of batch 1+2 (2026-11-02).** Three guards of this class were missing. (a) *An expected failure is no gate.* The latency e2e marked every over-budget case `test.fail`, which passes whatever the margin: a tap going from 150 to 600 ms stayed green, and a case near the budget that passed on a fast runner failed the run (an expected failure that passes). Missed because `test.fail` was read as "pending until fixed", not as "accepts any result". **Rule.** A budget not met yet gets a regression ceiling as an ordinary assertion (largest p75 of five isolated runs × 1.25, rounded up to 10 ms; lowered as owner tasks land, never raised), and the gap to the budget is an annotation naming the owner; `test.fail` never guards a number. Guard: `CEILINGS` in `e2e/latency.e2e.ts`, documented in docs/testing.md; the cases no longer run `serial`, so one past its ceiling does not hide the others' numbers. (b) *A failure path tested at one layer only.* Task 2.5's refused local write and refused pulled page were pinned at outbox level (`state.test.ts`) but not at the network, which CLAUDE.md's contract rule asks for: no test showed the network going quiet afterwards. Missed because the reviewer of each task read the code path and judged it correct, and correct code was taken as tested. **Rule.** Every failure path of the store gets a sync-level test that counts requests after the failure: the refused action is never pushed, a refused page is pulled again once from the same cursor and then left, a dependent orphan is pushed once. Guard: `createSyncClient after the local store refused a write` in `packages/client/src/sync-client.test.ts` (each case fails under its mutation: cursor moved before the write, no `markSynced`, refusal answered `ok`). (c) *A dropped answer.* Ten settings controls called `void actions.…()`: before Task 2.5 a refused write was an unhandled rejection and the control never moved; after it, the control flips and flips back with no word. Missed because the sweep of Task 2.5 looked for awaits before `integrate`, not for callers that ignore the `Result`, and jest-expo tests never refuse a write. **Rule.** An action's answer is never dropped: `void run(actions.…)` through `useRunAction`, which shows the refusal's sentence. Swept: Week's night-hours switch, Settings' language and night hours, the notification switches, digest times and quiet hours, the zone button, a project's archive, and the series auto-log on Day (`ensureInstances`, a count, stays). Guard: a `no-restricted-syntax` ban on `void actions.…()` and `void x.actions.…()` in `apps/app/src` (`ACTION_ANSWER_SYNTAX`, pinned by `scripts/eslint-scoped-bans.test.ts`), and app tests that refuse the write through `#app/test/refusing-store.ts` (Settings, Week).

### 109. Text from data in a flex row without a shrink rule
- **Bug.** Review 05 (break-ui, F-DAT-01/02/07): React Native gives every View `flexShrink: 0`, so a project name, an activity label or an address from data runs past its box or under a control, a truncated text shows its full value nowhere, and a label built from a list (the submit button) grows with the list. Task 1.4's sweep found the same break in the running tag, the sidebar header and the Week labels on realistic data, not only on the worst case.
- **Missed because.** Every fixture was short, one language and one width; the design-shots matrix looks at screenshots, not at boxes.
- **Rule.** Text from data yields (spec 4.1.2) and every truncation says so with `data-text-policy="truncate"` and its full value. The guard is `e2e/worst-case.e2e.ts` over the dev gallery (`/dev/gallery`, fixtures in `packages/core/src/testing/worst-case.ts`): every overflowing element must be in its `KNOWN` list with a finding id, and an entry that no longer matches fails, so the task that fixes a break deletes the entry. A new component or screen gets a gallery section in the same change.
- **Instance (QA round 22 R22-01): a clamp meant for data cut a sentence of the UI.** The toast
  clamped its whole message to three lines (`numberOfLines={3}`, there for a quoted task title), and
  on a computer it docks in the sidebar's ~200 px column: the sentence Task 2.5 added for a refused
  write («Не удалось сохранить это на устройстве, поэтому ничего не изменилось. Попробуйте снова.»,
  five lines there) lost its end, the one sentence that says why a tap was taken back. **Missed
  because** every refusal test read the sentence from the tree (`getByText`), which holds every word
  whatever the clamp; the gallery had no toast, and the overflow sweep measured width only, never
  text cut off at the bottom. **Rule.** A limit belongs to a value from data, never to the sentence
  around it (spec 4.1.2 rule 3): UI text wraps in full, a quoted value is cut at its slot
  (`toastQuote`: 48 graphemes, by grapheme through core's `clipText`). **Sweep.** Every toast that
  quotes data now quotes it short (a done task, a deleted task, an attended event, the assistant's
  approval lines); the export file name (generated, short) and the crash path (needed in full) stay
  whole; every toast sentence of its own shows whole (`actionError.*`, `settings.push.notSaved`,
  `notifications.failedAction`, `phone.ruleSaved`, `crash.saved`). Other UI copy under a line limit,
  owned by their kit tasks (spec 4.1.2: chrome labels wrap to two lines): the tab labels
  (`tab-bar.tsx`, Task 3.10), the time-bar tile labels (`time-bar.tsx`, Task 4.2), Insights' row
  names (Task 4.9). **Guard.** `toast.test.tsx` (no line limit on the message; the three longest
  sentences in both languages; `toastQuote` length), the gallery's Toast section at the docked and
  the phone width, and `e2e/worst-case.e2e.ts`, which fails any toast line cut off at the bottom
  (never a known break).
- **Fix round 1 of QA 22 (review).** Quoting whole lines broke the rule the instance set: the
  approval toast clipped each done line (cutting the sentence and its closing quote) and listed up
  to ten of them (about 25 lines in the dock, over the sidebar's links, class 6). The name is now
  quoted at its slot inside the sentence (`doneLines(…, quote)` quotes the subject and the new name)
  and the message names two actions and counts the rest (`approvalText`, «и ещё 3 действия», plural
  in both languages); the gallery's Toast section carries a ten-action approval, so the overflow
  sweep reads it.
- **Kit guard (Task 3.2, F-DAT-02).** The shrink rule is no longer each call site's to remember: the
  kit's `Text` (`ui/text.tsx`) is `flexShrink: 1, minWidth: 0` by construction and takes a `policy`
  (`wrap` by default, so UI strings are never cut; `truncate`; `clamp` with `lines`) instead of
  `numberOfLines`; on the web a cut text carries `data-text-policy` and its full value in the `title`
  (`text.web.tsx`), which is what the overflow sweep accepts. Controls stay `shrink-0` (the kit's
  `Pressable`). **Guard.** `pace/kit-primitives` (`rawLineLimit`): `numberOfLines` on React Native's
  `Text` outside `ui/text.tsx` is reported, with the way to the kit in the message (and `rawIcon`:
  a `lucide-react-native` import outside `ui/icon.tsx`, the same "reach past the kit" shape for
  icons, F-CMP-21); a warning held by the exact ratchet `KIT_PRIMITIVE_WARNINGS` (71 at
  introduction: 23 line limits, 48 icon imports), an error at 0; `text.test.tsx`,
  `text.web.test.tsx`, `kit-primitives.test.ts`, `eslint-scoped-bans.test.ts`. **Sweep.** The 23
  line limits the rule found are the class's remaining instances; the sidebar's owner name moved to
  the kit's `Text` (its worst-case entry F-DAT-07 "🌿 Bartholomew" left `e2e/worst-case.e2e.ts`), the
  rest, `ColorTag` and every tag and label in a row first, move in Task 3.9 and the 4.x screen tasks,
  each lowering the ratchet. **Fix round 1.** The icon half of the sweep is done (every lucide import
  through `Icon`, `rawIcon` 0, and the rule now sees re-exports, deep and dynamic imports); 13 line
  limits moved to the kit `Text`, 10 remain counted (`KIT_PRIMITIVE_WARNINGS` 10). A cut text built of
  parts takes its full value as `full`, so the sweep can read it; without it the text stays unmarked and
  the sweep reports it.

### 110. Locale formatting by hand
- **Bug.** Design review 05 (F-CPY-04): Insights showed «3.3» and «0.0» in Russian (`toFixed(1)`), a count of 1 284 read «1284» (`String(count)`), Russian durations were «23 ч 58 м» (a breaking space, «м» reads as metres) and `formatDuration` never rolled hours into days («Оценка 168 ч») while `formatLeft` did. The catalog held «15 минут» and «2,5 ч» with plain spaces, so a unit could land alone at the start of a line.
- **Missed because.** Every fixture and every test ran in English, where `toFixed`, `String(n)` and a plain space are right. The RU catalog test compared keys and placeholders, never the text next to a number; a number built in code never passed a locale at all.
- **Rule.** A number the person reads is written by core: `formatNumber` (decimal separator, grouping with U+202F in Russian), `formatCount`, `formatPercent`, `formatDuration` / `formatLeft` / `formatSpan` (U+00A0 between number and unit, a plain space between the two pairs so a long value wraps only there; «2 ч 05 мин»; days from 48 h). A `{count}` given to `t()` as a number is grouped by `t()` itself, and `plural()` groups its count. Sweep: Insights tiles, the count badge, folded sections, the Now group counts, the project figures, the gallery counts, and nine catalog texts. Guards: `no-restricted-syntax` on `.toFixed(` in `apps/app/src`, and an i18n test that fails a Russian catalog text with a digit, a plain space and a unit (`мин`, `ч`, `час`, `сек`, `дн`, `мес`, `%`). New code asks "what does this read like in Russian, at 1 284, at 168 h?".
- **Review of batch 1+2 (2026-11-02).** (a) Past 48 h `formatDuration` floored the hours, and it also prints tracked totals: 49 h 59 min showed «2 дн 1 ч», up to 59 minutes gone. Now the exact value rounds to the nearest hour, half up (2 d 4 h 30 min → «2 дн 5 ч»); `formatLeft`, `formatSpan` and `formatLate` keep flooring (a countdown never claims more time than is left; an age or a delay reads "at least"). (b) The `toFixed` / first-character bans covered only `apps/app/src`, while `packages/client/src/view-models` builds display text too: they now cover both, tests aside, pinned by `scripts/eslint-scoped-bans.test.ts` (no hits in view-models).

### 111. Text sliced by UTF-16 code unit
- **Bug.** Design review 05 (F-DAT-08): the avatar's initials came from `part.slice(0, 1)`, which takes one UTF-16 code unit. For a name that starts with an emoji («🦊 Лиса») it drew a lone surrogate, a broken glyph, beside «Л»; a name with no letter drew a digit or a bracket.
- **Missed because.** Test names were ASCII or Cyrillic, all in the Basic Multilingual Plane, where a code unit is a character. No test fed a name from the real world of Telegram profiles (emoji, flags, combining marks).
- **Rule.** Text is cut by grapheme, never by `slice`/`charAt`/`substring`: `initials` (letters of the first two words; an emoji-first name shows that emoji alone; `""` means the person icon) and `capitalize` in core use `Intl.Segmenter`, with a regex fallback for Hermes. A property test asserts `initials` never returns a lone surrogate for arbitrary strings, and at most two graphemes. Sweep: the avatar and the calendar's month title (`charAt(0)`); the other `slice(0, 1)` hits were lists, not text. Guard: `no-restricted-syntax` on `.slice(0, 1)`, `.substring(0, 1)` and `.charAt(0)` in `apps/app/src`. Truncating long text with an ellipsis is a CSS job (`numberOfLines`), not a `slice`.

### 112. A codemod maps the name of a class, not the job it did
- **Bug.** Batch 1+2 visual review (`context/design/reviews/10-batch-2-visual.md`): Task 2.2 mapped `accentFg` → `accent.on`, `bg-project-*` → the hue's tint and `accentQuiet` → `accent.quiet` by name, as §9.2 says. But the old class did a job the new one cannot: `accentFg` also painted a switch knob, which `react-native-web` draws wider than its track, so a dark knob (`accent.on` is near-black in dark) vanished into the dark page (V2-01); the solid project fill was the *selected* cue of a chip, and a 12 % tint under the same ring is 1.1:1 (V2-02); the solid accent badge became a pale disc (V2-03); `faint` (a data bar's grey) became `label.quaternary`, the disabled role (V2-04). The focus ring and the sidebar ground were never mapped at all (the tokens existed, nothing used them).
- **Missed because.** The contrast test measures pairs of roles, and every role pair was fine (`accent.on` on `accent` is 6.9:1); nothing tied a *component's* state to the pair it must draw, and a component that draws a knob outside its track has no pair at all. jest renders no pixels; the re-baseline compared layout, not whether a selected thing still looks selected.
- **Rule.** A token map is checked by the job of each use, not by the name: for every mapped class, ask what it told the person (selected, on, count, data, disabled, focus) and which pair carries that; a selected, on or counted state is a fill against its ground at ≥ 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11), never a tint; a data mark never takes a disabled role; a control draws its parts inside its own box, so the page behind it cannot swallow them.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept in this change: every selected state (chips, Segmented, the calendar's picked day), the switch, the count badge (tab bar, sidebar, bell: `tone`), Insights' bars (the only `label.quaternary` non-text use), the sidebar ground and every focus ring (one `:focus-visible` rule in `global.css`; the rest of the CSS draws none). Guards: the cue pairs in `USED_PAIRS` (`accent` against all five grounds, the off knob on its track, a neutral badge, the slate bar on its track), component tests that pin the roles each state resolves to (`chip`, `switch-row`, `count-badge`, `calendar`, `sidebar`, `tab-bar`, Insights) and a stylesheet test that the only ring is in the `focus` role (`focus-ring.test.ts`). QA checklist: a changed state is looked at in both themes next to its sibling state.
- **Instance (batch 1+2 close-out).** Task 2.2 mapped the selected task row's `bg-raised` to `fill.3`
  (the "selected-neutral" role), but the row's job was to carry its whole meta line on that ground:
  `label.tertiary` times fell to 3.96:1 (page) / 4.30:1 (cell) in light, the danger word to 4.0, every
  tag's ink on its tint to 4.2-4.7 (axe in `composer.e2e.ts` and `journeys.e2e.ts`). The OAuth client
  card had the same `raised` → `fill.3` with a tertiary and a warning line. `ContrastPair` measured one
  layer, so a tag on a selected row (tint over selection over page) had no pair. Now the row's ground is
  `SELECTED_ROW_FILL` (`fill.1`, the only neutral fill every meta role passes on), `USED_PAIRS` measures
  each meta role and every hue's tag on it (`between` layers the selection), `task-row.test.tsx` pins
  the class to it, and the client card is a `bg-cell` panel (`oauth.test.tsx`). Spec §4's rebuilt row
  (Task 4.1) asks for `selection` + a leading bar: `selection` fails the same pairs (tertiary 3.7:1 on
  the page), so Task 4.1 changes `SELECTED_ROW_FILL` and lets the pair test decide.

### 113. A library's default taken as the platform's behaviour
- **Bug.** Review of the batch 1+2 visual fix: the kit switch became a `Pressable` with role `switch`, and Space no longer toggled it on the web. react-native-web 0.21 presses on Space only for `role="button"` (or a `<button>`); its own `Switch` rendered a real checkbox, which the browser toggles on Space, so the old switch did it for free. The same hole sat in every radio, checkbox and switch `Pressable` (chips, the check circle, project and OS pickers, colour swatches, subtasks, scopes, Week's moon). Next to it, a ring rule written for the page (`:focus-visible`) lost to an older `input:focus { outline: none }` by specificity, so the range slider lost its keyboard ring.
- **Missed because.** jest renders no browser: no key reaches a handler, no cascade is computed. The e2e tests pressed with the mouse or with Enter, and the one keyboard test only asked that the outline not be `none`, not in which colour.
- **Rule.** Replacing a native control with a drawn one inherits none of the native keyboard contract: write down what the native one did (Space, Enter, arrows, focus ring) and give the drawn one the same, on the web through `spacePress` (`ui/space-press.web.ts`). A page-wide rule is checked in the browser, against the computed style of each kind of control it is meant to reach, not by reading the stylesheet.
- **Sweep and guard.** Every `Pressable` with role radio, checkbox or switch now spreads `spacePress`; the input rule excludes the slider. Guards: `space-press.web.test.ts`, `e2e/keyboard.e2e.ts` (Space on a switch and a chip; the computed ring of a switch and of the range slider is the focus role), a stylesheet test that no input-hiding rule reaches the slider. Disabled controls get no action: `spacePress(onPress, isDisabled)` (a sent subtask is `disabled`, react-native-web leaves it click-focusable and the browser still delivers Space, which revoked its solved mark), guarded by `task-screen.test.tsx` and `space-press-callers.test.ts` (a file that disables a control passes the state). `spacePress` goes on radio, checkbox, switch and tab roles, never on `role="button"` or a native `<button>` (they press on Space already: it would fire twice); the phone's tab bar has it too.

### 114. An autofix that changes what the code means
- **Bug.** pr48 follow-ups (implementers, round 7): `bun lint` turned the drain's getter
  `() => inFlight` (a `Promise | undefined`) into `async () => await inFlight`. The getter never
  answered `undefined` again, so `drain`'s loop `run !== undefined` never ended and `sync.hold`
  hung. `@typescript-eslint/promise-function-async` reports a "hybrid" return (a promise or
  something else) and still inserts `async`; `return-await` (`always`) then adds the `await`.
- **Missed because.** The fix runs inside `bun lint`, which every change runs and nobody reads as a
  code change; the sync tests caught it only because a hold happened to be tested. Nothing tested
  the lint config's fixes themselves.
- **Rule.** An autofix may only change form, never meaning. A rule whose fix can change what a
  function returns (adds `async`, unwraps, narrows) keeps its report and loses that fix: run it
  through a `pace/` wrapper that drops the fix for the case, never an inline disable. A function
  that answers a promise or something else carries an explicit return type (the upstream message
  says so).
- **Sweep and guard.** `pace/promise-function-async` (`scripts/eslint-rules/promise-function-async.ts`)
  runs the upstream rule and drops its fix for `missingAsyncHybridReturn`; its RuleTester pins
  "reported, output unchanged" and a config test pins that the upstream rule stays off. Swept:
  `return-await`'s fix only adds `await` inside an `async` function (no change of meaning on its
  own). The config's many other fixers (unicorn, perfectionist, stylistic) were not audited one by
  one: a reviewer reads what `bun lint` changed in a diff like any other code.

### 115. A test's time budget set on an idle machine
- **Bug.** pr48 follow-ups (implementers): `ids-no-prng.test.ts` timed out once under load. Each
  of its tests loads `ids.ts` and ulidx afresh after `vi.resetModules()`; the cold transform took
  more than vitest's 5 s default while the rest of the suite ran.
- **Missed because.** The suite is written and run on a machine with time to spare; a cold import
  costs milliseconds there. `initials.test.ts` had met the same thing and got its own timeout,
  but nothing carried that to other files that import afresh.
- **Rule.** A test whose work grows with the machine's load (a fresh import of a module graph, a
  real worker start) states its own timeout, sized for a loaded CI runner, next to a line saying why.
- **Sweep and guard.** Every test file that calls `vi.resetModules()` and imports afresh
  (`ids-no-prng`, `numbers`, `initials`); `scripts/fresh-import-timeout.test.ts` fails a new one
  without a `timeout:`.

### 116. A test green alone, red in its suite
- **Bug.** A8's RuleTester (`promise-function-async.test.ts`) passed when run by itself and failed
  inside `bun test:unit`. The type-aware cases had no `filename`, so typescript-eslint linted them
  as `estree.ts`, a path that does not exist on disk. In the full suite the shared project service
  had already been warmed by the other rule tests and answered that path "not found by the project
  service".
- **Missed because.** The red/green cycle ran the one file only; the suite's entry point
  (`bun test:unit`, one Bun process over `scripts`) ran later.
- **Rule.** Before committing, a new test passes the way CI runs it (the package's script), not
  only alone. A type-aware lint test names a real file the project includes (`filename`).
- **Sweep and guard.** The other RuleTester (`no-raw-design-values.test.ts`) is not type-aware and
  names its files. `verification-before-completion` runs the suite scripts, not the single file.

### 117. A destructive action that does not look at what it would destroy
- **Bug.** QA round 23 R23-02: Log out wipes the local copy, outbox included. Offline, a change
  the pill had just promised to sync was dropped with no word.
- **Missed because.** The tests of Log out ran on a synced device, where the copy holds nothing
  the server lacks; "offline" was tested for writes and the pill, never for what leaving does.
- **Rule.** Before an action deletes local state, it asks whether that state holds anything that
  exists nowhere else (an outbox, a draft, an unsent upload); if so it says how much and offers a
  way to keep it. Test each destructive action with that state non-empty.
- **Sweep and guard.** Both Log out buttons (Settings, the versions screen) share `LogoutButton`.
  The half-typed line is already per-account by design (class 103). `settings.test.tsx` pins it.

### 118. A follow-up after a network await that assumes the screen has not moved
- **Bug.** PR A re-review N1: Log out awaited `auth.logout()` and then called
  `router.replace("/login")`. The client signs out locally before it sends the POST, so the route
  gate had already shown the login card; when a slow answer came back, the replace to the route
  already shown remounted it and emptied the id the person had started typing (the stale-tab e2e's
  flake, class 75 in fix round 1, was this line, not a session race).
- **Missed because.** The jest tests asserted the replace was called, which is the bug; the e2e
  logged out against a fast local Worker, so the answer nearly always came before the typing.
  Nothing held the answer back.
- **Rule.** What a state change already does (the gate's redirect on `signed-out`) is not done again
  after an await: a follow-up placed after a network call runs on whatever screen the person reached
  meanwhile. Navigation that follows a state change belongs to the one place that watches the state.
  Test a flow with its answer held (`page.route` with a delay) while the person acts on the next
  screen.
- **Sweep and guard.** Deleting the account (`delete-account.tsx`) and ending this device's own
  session (`sessions-section.tsx`) also navigated to `/login` after signing out; both now leave it
  to the gate. `login-navigation.test.ts` fails when a file outside the gate and the bare sign-in
  screens names the `/login` route; `login.e2e.ts` "a slow log-out answer…" holds the answer 2 s.

### 119. A test-only sign-in path that stands in for the real one
