- **Bug.** Owner bug 2026-11-03: connecting Claude (an MCP client) failed. The consent page asked
  for the Telegram widget even though the browser already held the person's Pace session, and on
  the owner's network the widget's frame loaded a block page, clipped over the text below, so
  Allow never enabled. `POST /api/oauth/complete` accepted only a widget payload or a dev id.
- **Missed because.** Every e2e test and QA round signed in on the consent page with the dev id
  (`EXPO_PUBLIC_DEV_LOGIN`, `DevIdentity`), so the real identity step never ran: not the widget,
  not its frame on a network that blocks it, not the "already signed in" case. No QA round
  connected a real MCP client end to end, and the journey "connect Claude from a browser where I
  am signed in" was never walked (docs/design.md step 1). The decision behind it (plan.md: widget
  on the web origin) asked how to prove identity, never whether it was already proved.
- **Rule (the testing class).** A shortcut that exists for tests (a dev id, a seeded token, a mocked
  widget) never stands in for the real path in the one test of a journey: each journey that proves
  who the person is gets at least one test that runs the path a real person meets, signed in and
  signed out, and the release round walks it with a real client.
- **The product half is class 81.** "Asked to sign in while already signed in" is the system asking
  the person for what it already has (class 81: a key the system needs asked of the person); the
  consent page's fix (use the session) is filed there in spirit, and the sweep below is its sweep.
- **Guard: procedural first.** The real identity step cannot run in CI (Telegram's widget, a real
  client's token exchange), so the guard for the testing half is the owner's release step in
  `docs/qa.md` ("Release round": connect a real MCP client end to end). The e2e below pins what can
  be pinned without Telegram: the signed-in path sends no dev id or Telegram proof. The frame half
  of the same bug is class 121.
- **Sweep and guard.** Every place that asks for a Telegram proof: the web login (redirects a
  signed-in browser to `next`, hands its token to the app for `?return=app`), the consent page (the
  one that asked a signed-in browser; now uses the session), the Android app's login (shown only
  without a session), the account-deletion confirmation (a bot confirmation of a destructive
  action by design, not a sign-in). `e2e/oauth-consent.e2e.ts`: signed in, Allow with no widget
  (bearer, no Telegram proof); a session revoked while the page is open falls back to signing in and
  the network goes quiet; signed out, the app's own sign-in comes back with the request intact.
  `docs/qa.md`, "Release round": the owner connects a real MCP client end to end.

### 120. A runner's behaviour that only CI's environment switches on
- **Bug.** Fix round 3 (A9 review, Recommendation 3): CI stopped at `bun test:unit` with ".only is
  disabled in CI environments": the two RuleTester files assigned `RuleTester.itOnly = it.only`,
  and Bun's runner throws on reading `it.only` when `CI` is set. Every later CI step (API, app,
  e2e) never ran for the PR.
- **Missed because.** Every local run (the implementers' and the reviewers') ran without `CI`, where
  Bun allows `.only`; the assignment focused nothing, so nothing looked wrong. Nobody read the CI
  result of the head before reporting it green.
- **Rule.** A behaviour a runner switches on from the environment (`CI`: Bun's `.only` refusal,
  vitest's `allowOnly`, Playwright's `forbidOnly`, retries, workers) is part of the gate: run the
  suites the way CI runs them (`CI=1 bun test:unit`) before calling them green, and read the CI run
  of the head before a report says it passes. No test is focused in a commit, by any spelling.
- **Sweep and guard.** The two RuleTester files were the only uses (`it.only`, `describe.only`,
  `test.only`, `itOnly`, `only: true` across every unit, Worker, script and e2e test).
  `scripts/no-focused-tests.test.ts` reads every test file and fails on any of those spellings
  (red before: these two lines), whatever the environment.

### 121. Content we do not control laid out without a box of its own
- **Bug.** Owner bug 2026-11-03 (the frame half; A9 review I2): the Telegram widget's frame, which
  on the owner's network loaded a block page instead of the button, sat in a flex box sized for
  the button with no width limit and no clip; the frame spilled past the consent card and over the
  text below it.
- **Missed because.** Every test ran the widget either from the real script (offline: nothing
  loads) or not at all; nothing ever put a frame of another size where the button goes. Telegram's
  button is small, so the box looked right in every shot.
- **Rule.** A third-party frame, embed or injected node (a login widget, a map, a video, an ad)
  gets a box of its own: full width of its column at most (`max-width: 100%` on what is injected),
  clipped, in the flow, so whatever it loads pushes the page down and never draws over it. Test it
  with a stub that loads something larger than the real thing, and assert it stays inside its card
  and clear of the controls next to it.
- **Sweep and guard.** The Telegram widget is the only third-party frame the app renders; the web
  login shares the component and its box. `e2e/oauth-consent.e2e.ts` "a frame the widget loads
  stays in its own box…" stubs `telegram-widget.js` with a 600 × 300 frame at 390 px: inside the
  card, clear of "Can't see the Telegram button?" and its button, which still takes the click (red
  against the old layout: the frame started 105 px left of the screen).

### 122. A cross-cutting state drawn by every call site on its own
- **Bug.** Review F-CMP-01 (redesign spec §4.1.1): 21 of the app's 63 pressables gave no feedback at all
  when pressed, the rest drew it each in its own way (43 `active:opacity-*`, four disabled opacities), and
  none had a hover or an Android ripple. NativeWind's `active:` is a React state, so even the feedback that
  existed came a render after the finger, and react-native-web held press-in back another 50 ms.
- **Missed because.** Each control was written and tested on its own, for what it does when pressed, never
  for what the person sees while pressing; jest draws no pixels and no test listed the pressables to ask
  each the same question. Nothing made the shared state the default: a bare React Native `Pressable` was as
  easy to reach as a kit one.
- **Rule.** A state every control must show (pressed, hover, focus, selected, disabled) is drawn once, by
  one kit primitive, and a call site cannot opt out by reaching past it: the raw primitive is banned by
  lint outside the kit, with the way to the kit in the message. Feedback that must be instant is drawn by
  the platform (a native ripple, a CSS `:active`), never by a React state.
- **Sweep and guard.** `ui/pressable.tsx` (+ `.web.tsx`) is the state layer: Android ripple in `fill.3`,
  web `[data-pace-press]` in `global.css` (hover `fill.1` only for a hovering pointer, pressed `fill.2` in
  0 ms and out `duration.xs`, forced colours), `delayPressIn={0}` on the web, haptics through
  `platform/haptics.ts`. Guards: `no-restricted-imports` of React Native's `Pressable`/`Touchable*` in
  `src/features/**` and `src/shared/**` (a warning held by the exact ratchet `BARE_PRESSABLE_WARNINGS`, 33
  at introduction, lowered as the kit tasks migrate each caller, then an error); `pace/no-raw-design-values`
  reports any `active:` class outside `ui/pressable.tsx` as `pressedClass` (the 46 that exist were already
  counted in `DESIGN_VALUE_WARNINGS`); `pressable.test.tsx`, `pressable.web.test.tsx` and
  `state-layer.test.ts` (the CSS held to the motion tokens). Fix round 1: Pressable also owns Space for
  tab, checkbox, radio and switch (class 113, the disabled gate in one place; a caller that still spreads
  `spacePress` is not pressed twice), says `selected` only on roles ARIA allows it and `checked` on
  checkables, draws nothing for a Pressable with no press, and the web overlay is the control's `::after`.
  The callers (Button, IconButton, Chip, rows)
  move in their own tasks (redesign plan 3.x); `src/screens`, `src/auth` and `src/platform` are not under
  the import ban yet (four files).

### 123. Contrast asserted for text only
- **Bug.** Review F-COL-09 (and F-COL-01/02, findings "Bugs" 32): the off switch's knob was the
  surface colour on the surface (1.0:1 in dark, white on a white card in light), so on and off differed
  by the track's colour alone; the selected row read 1.10:1 and field edges 1.25-1.35:1. Task 3.9 met the
  class again while building it: the segmented control's chosen thumb (`bg.elevated` on the `fill.2`
  track) measures 1.05-1.15:1, and an `outline` edge on that track 2.5-2.9:1 in light.
- **Missed because.** The contrast test measured the text tokens on the grounds, the pairs a reviewer
  thinks of as "colours". A knob, a thumb, a ring, a disc or a fill is a mark that carries a state, but
  no pair named it, so nothing measured it: a mark on a translucent fill over a ground is a composite no
  one computes by eye.
- **Rule.** Every mark a control draws to carry a state (a knob, a thumb, a check, a selected fill, a
  track's fill, an edge) is a pair in `USED_PAIRS` at the `ui` target (WCAG 1.4.11, 3:1) on the real
  composite it sits on, added in the same change as the mark. A mark that cannot reach 3:1 on its fill
  gets an edge that can (the segmented thumb's `label.tertiary` hairline, the switch's `label.tertiary`
  knob), never "it is only decoration" while it is the state's cue.
- **Sweep (Task 3.9).** Every control of the kit: the switch (off knob `label.tertiary` on `fill.2`, on
  knob `accent.on` on `accent`: present), the chip and the segment at rest (`label.secondary` and
  `label.primary` on `fill.2`: added), the destructive button (the spec's `status.dangerQuiet`
  fill under the danger label read 4.31:1 over the page in axe, `sync-state.e2e.ts`' offline Log out;
  the pair existed only over `bg.cell`: the button is now `bg.cell` inside a danger ring, on its
  `bg.cell` pair), the chosen segment's thumb (edge: added via the switch's pair), the
  checked circle (`bg.app` on `label.secondary`: added), the slider's thumb and fill (`label.primary` on
  the grounds and on `fill.4`: added), the progress fill (`label.primary`, the hue strokes and slate on
  `fill.4`: present for slate, primary added), the chosen chip (`accent` on the grounds: present), the
  count badges (present). Disabled marks are exempt (WCAG). Left for their tasks: the selected row's fill
  (Task 3.3 carries the accent bar as the cue), field edges (Task 3.8).
- **Guard.** `packages/core/src/design/contrast.test.ts` over `USED_PAIRS` (both themes); the kit's
  component tests pin each mark's class to the role its pair names (`switch.test.tsx`,
  `segmented.test.tsx`, `check-circle.test.tsx`, `chip.test.tsx`), so a recolour without a pair fails one
  of the two.

### 124. A parent claiming to be the page
- **Bug.** QA round 23 R23-14 (and a PR #48 follow-up): on Presets and New preset the sidebar drew
  Settings as the current page (the full selected look and `aria-current="page"`) while the screen itself
  showed Back to Settings. A screen reader heard "Settings, current page" on a page that was not Settings.
- **Missed because.** A destination knew only `owns(path)`: "this path is mine". The tests asked whether
  the right row was highlighted, never whether the highlight said page or place; the pushed screens a
  destination owns (a task under Now, Presets under Settings) had no row of their own to be checked against.
- **Rule.** Navigation tells three things apart: its own page (`aria-current="page"`, the full current
  look), a screen it owns but does not name (`aria-current="true"`, a quieter look that still says where
  the person is), and elsewhere. A parent never claims to be the page a child shows.
- **Sweep and guard.** `currentOf(destination, path)` in `ui/sidebar.tsx` serves the sidebar's `NavItem`
  and the rail; the tab bar's current tab is its own route. `nav-item.test.tsx` pins the three looks and
  a table of paths (Settings on `/presets` and `/presets/new`, Now on a task and `/add`).

### 125. A live region around a ticking value
- **Bug.** PR B 4.2 (F-A11Y-10): the running row sat in `accessibilityLiveRegion="polite"` around the
  elapsed time, which changes every minute: a screen reader read the whole row at each tick.
- **Missed because.** Tests asserted what was shown, never what is *announced*; a live region looks
  like a feature ("polite") and nothing counts how often it speaks.
- **Rule.** A value that changes while the screen is idle (a clock, a counter, a progress) is never
  inside a live region. Its transitions that matter (started, stopped, past the Expect) are announced
  once each with `AccessibilityInfo.announceForAccessibility`; what is on screen at mount is not news.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept every `accessibilityLiveRegion` in `apps/app/src`: the remaining ones wrap
  one-off messages (a reading status, an error). `running-card.test.tsx` pins "not a live region" and
  "announces a start, a stop and past-expected once each"; QA checklist line in `docs/qa.md` scope:
  "does anything that ticks speak?".


### 126. A focus asked for while the field cannot take it
- **Bug.** QA round 21 M2 / round 23 R23-12: a direct load of `/add` opened the New task form, whose
  title asked for the cursor at mount — under a fresh browser's first-sync cover, where the field is
  hidden and takes no focus. Focus stayed on `BODY` (a phone and a computer alike). The composer's
  line already waited for the cover (QA 19 L4); the form's title did not.
- **Missed because.** Tests rendered the form with the copy already synced, so "focus at mount" always
  worked; the cover exists only in the first-sync state.
- **Rule.** Every "focus on arrival" waits for what can hide the field: the first-sync cover
  (`useIsFirstSync`), an overlay still entering. Ask once it can be seen.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept `focusField(` / `autoFocus` callers: the line (done), `TaskFields` title
  (fixed), the add-computer field (not under the cover). `screens/add-focus.test.tsx` holds the first
  pull and asserts no focus before the cover lifts and one after; `e2e/composer.e2e.ts` checks the
  title is focused on a direct load of `/add` at 390 and 1440.

### 127. A choice offered that the validation always refuses
- **Bug.** QA round 21 L3: the activity sheet's "End day" chips ran to the day after the old end; for a block that ended today that was tomorrow, and any time on it was answered "That is still ahead".
- **Missed because.** The chips and the check were written apart: the chips from the block's own dates, the check from `now`; tests used a block far in the past, where the two never disagree.
- **Rule.** Options are derived from the same rule that validates them: if a value can only ever be refused, it is not offered. The test clock sits at the edge (a day that has just begun, one that has not).
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the other chip sets built from dates (`DayChoices` start days, Week's detail: none). `activity-draft.test.ts` pins the edge in Moscow time.

### 128. Text from data placed in chrome without a length policy
- **Bug.** PR B 4.5 (F-DAT-01): the task footer's submit button was built from the names of every solved
  subtask; with 37 of them it grew to 20 lines and at 320 px the body was 0 px tall.
- **Missed because.** Fixtures had two or three short subtasks, and jest-expo draws no layout, so a label
  that wraps looks the same as one that fits.
- **Rule.** A button, tab, badge or bar title is chrome: its length is set by a policy (`fixed` words plus a
  `count`, or one truncated line), never by data. The full data goes to the accessibility hint and to the
  sheet the button opens. The pure function that builds such a label (`submitLabel`) is tested with the
  worst case (37 items, a 200-character name), not with the typical one.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept every `t(...)` call that interpolates a list or a name into a `Button` or an
  `IconButton` label: the toasts already quote through `toastQuote`; the close sheet's title keeps the
  full list on purpose (it scrolls). Guard: `submitLabel` tests in `task.test.ts`.

### 129. A control replaced by its own confirm drops the way back
- **Bug.** PR B 4.5 (F-A11Y-02): the task header's trash deleted at once, the page stayed as «No such
  task», and Escape did nothing in the confirm that other menus had.
- **Missed because.** The delete test pressed the button and checked the store; no test pressed Escape or
  Cancel, so nothing asserted that the question could be refused from the keyboard.
- **Rule.** A destructive item asks in place (the item turns into «Delete the task?» with Cancel and
  Delete, focus on Cancel), Escape cancels the question before it closes the menu, and the recovery is
  offered at once. The test walks Cancel, Escape and Delete in that order.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the other one-tap destructive controls (sessions, History undo, wipe): they
  already confirm. Guard: the `deleting` tests in `task-screen.test.tsx`.

### 130. A box drawn at a minimum size hangs outside the clip that holds it
- **Bug.** PR B 4.6 (the day's-end block, found by the rebase): a 3-minute item at 23:56 is drawn at the 24 px
  target and ran past the day column, which the sideways scroller clips, so half of its target missed taps;
  lifting it into the column would have covered the item before it.
- **Missed because.** Geometry (drawn height) and lanes were computed in two places and the grid's tests never
  put an item at the edge of the column; jest draws no clip.
- **Rule.** One function (`layoutWeek`) returns each box's drawn rectangle, its lane and how far the lowest box
  reaches below the column (`overhang`); the grid keeps that much room under the last hour, so a minimum-size
  box never leaves its clip and never moves onto a neighbour. A pure test places the item at the edge.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the other minimum-size boxes (Day's rail, the time bar tiles): none is clipped by an
  ancestor. Guard: `week-layout.test.ts` (5 lanes, the 23:56 item, folds) and the e2e in `week.e2e.ts` that
  asserts the target is inside the scroller and covers no neighbour.

### 131. One sentence for several reasons, in the one place that was not swept
- **Bug.** QA round 21 L7, closed in PR B 4.7: the rules' preview card said «Assistant unavailable» for
  out of requests, unreachable and «this server has no assistant» alike, while the composer's form had a
  sentence for each (bug class 99 had fixed the form and the no-card line, not the card).
- **Missed because.** Class 99's sweep named the screens that show a reading issue; the card's own
  two-way `?:` (unreadable or everything else) looked complete, and its test used one reason.
- **Rule.** A text keyed by a reason is looked up by the reason itself (`assistant.rulesUsed.${reason}`), so
  a new reason is a type error until its sentence exists in both catalogs, never a fall-through branch.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept every `reason === …` ternary over `ParseUnavailableReason`. Guard: `it.each`
  over the reasons in `composer.test.tsx`; the typed key.

### 132. A control inside a pressable row is a button in a button
- **Bug.** PR B 4.7/4.10 (found while rebuilding the rows): an Inbox row that opens its details and also
  carries Accept/Delete, and a History row that asks before undoing and also carries Undo, cannot be one
  pressable (axe `nested-interactive`; TalkBack reads one button).
- **Missed because.** The old cards were not pressable, so no test could nest; the kit's `ListRow`
  documents `control` only for a leading check.
- **Rule.** A row with its own actions is a row beside its actions (the row presses, the actions are
  siblings), or it is not pressable and the action is the only way in; never both on one box. Buttons that
  repeat per row name their row («Accept: <text>»).
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the lists: Day's rows, Projects and the task rows keep one control per row.
  Guard: the Inbox and History tests press the row and the button separately by name.

### 133. A refusal said a screen below the field it is about
- **Bug.** QA round 23 R23-05, closed in PR B 4.8: a taken or empty preset name was refused in a line at the
  foot of a long form; the Name field showed nothing and kept no cursor.
- **Missed because.** The test read the alert anywhere on the screen (`findByRole("alert")`), so a message far
  from its field passed; jest has no viewport.
- **Rule.** A refusal about one field is that field's `error` (marked, announced, with the cursor sent back
  to it) and goes when the field's value changes; only a refusal about the whole form uses the form's line.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the forms: Day's sheet, the project form and the close sheet already attach
  theirs (the project form's name error stays a form line, by its single field). Guard: the preset tests
  assert the sentence, `aria-invalid` on the field and that editing the name clears it.

### 134. Owner-corrected copy with no test pinning it
- **Bug.** The update banner went back to "We changed a few things" (PR #42) after the owner had rejected that copy twice; "Settings → Devices" and the long Calendar hint came back the same way (F-CPY-01).
- **Missed because.** A test pinned the rejected copy itself (`update-banner.web.test.tsx` expected "We changed a few things"), and no test listed phrases the owner had banned or strings they had agreed.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the catalogs for the banned phrases (none left). Guard: `packages/core/src/i18n/glossary.test.ts` lists banned phrases, pins the agreed update copy and the Calendar hint's length, and holds one word per action per language (class 45). A correction by the owner adds its phrase there in the same change.

### 135. A test that assumes the host's time zone
- **Bug.** Tests passed on one machine and failed on another: `app-state.test.tsx` assumed the device was not in Moscow (0747db9), and `week.e2e.ts`'s 23:56 test never seeded the account zone while its neighbours passed `timezone: "UTC"`, so it depended on where it ran.
- **Missed because.** Every run so far was on a host whose zone was UTC or close to it; nothing varied the zone.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept `e2e/**` and the jest and vitest suites for host-zone and locale reads: the e2e project now pins `timezoneId: "UTC"` and `locale: "en-US"` (a test needing another zone sets its own, as `sync-state.e2e.ts`), and the 23:56 test seeds the account zone. The jest app suite (its `test` script sets `TZ`) and the core and client vitest configs run under `TZ=America/Los_Angeles`, so a UTC assumption fails everywhere; `tz-guard.test.ts` in core and a case in `settings.test.tsx` fail if the suite ends up in UTC again.

### 136. Optional descriptive metadata able to block a core flow
- **Bug.** The phone sends its maker, model, Android version and name at login. The Worker's strict body schema refused a value over its limit (40/60/20/80 characters; custom ROMs report long `Build.MODEL`), so the whole `POST /api/auth/nonce` answered 400/422 and a cosmetic field took away the only way to sign in on Android. The strict object also made a new app fail against an older Worker (final review of PR #54, I1 and M1).
- **Missed because.** Tests pinned the refusal as the wanted behaviour ("refuses an out-of-range device") and fed short fixtures; no test asked what a real phone sends, or what an older or newer peer sends.
- **Class.** Untrusted or peer-supplied optional metadata whose validation can fail the request it only decorates.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the auth and login bodies: every optional descriptive field of `NonceCreateSchema` (name, brand, model, OS version, and the install id) is now dropped when invalid (`droppedIfInvalid`), the object ignores unknown keys, and the client cuts the texts to `DEVICE_FIELD_LIMITS` by grapheme before sending (`cleanLoginDevice`) and asks again with the old fields on a 422 from an older Worker. Guards: core schema test, api int tests (too long, blank, wrong type, unknown key), client unit tests. `cloud.ts`'s upload `deviceName` is a required field of an authenticated upload, not a login, and stays strict. Rule: client-sent descriptive metadata never blocks auth.

### 137. The first e2e run of a whole redesign: specs written against the old screens, and two real faults they found
- **Bug.** The first CI run of the overhaul's e2e suite (217 tests) failed 45: specs still drove the old screens (Delete on the task page, a switch's ring on the control, `shell-content` once, sheets on a computer where the picker is now a popover, a `textbox` that is now a `searchbox`, 500+ events in one push); and two real faults: a popover with no known height opened below a bottom-edge tile and left the window (the time bar's pickers on a computer, 1440 × 900), and the unread dot was an unnamed `div` with an `aria-label` (axe `aria-prohibited-attr`).
- **Missed because.** Screen tasks ran jest and lint only (the owner's rule: e2e runs in CI), so no spec ran against a built screen until the whole PR was up; the popover took its height from a prop nobody passed and jest has no layout.
- **Class.** A shared overlay that needs its own size to be placed, taking it from an optional prop; a decorative marker given an accessible name without a role; specs that are not run on the commit that changes their screen.
- **Sweep and guard.** `Popover` measures its frame when no height is passed (every caller); the dot has `role="img"`; the specs' shared flows moved to `e2e/support` (`deleteOpenTask`). A screen task that changes a flow greps `e2e/` for its labels in the same commit.

### 138. A press visual that redraws under the pointer swallows the click
- **Bug.** On the web the check of a task row did nothing (journeys e2e, phone and desktop, red since PR B's first CI run). The check circle draws its tick on press-in; the new `<svg>` landed under the pointer, the mouseup hit it while the mousedown had hit the circle, and Chrome fired no `click`, which is what react-native-web's press listens for.
- **Missed because.** jest presses through `fireEvent`, never through the browser's pointer-to-click step; the e2e failure was read from screenshots ("cause not found") instead of from the events.
- **Class.** A control whose drawing changes on press-in (pressed-only icons, swapped children) inside a target the browser hit-tests.
- **Sweep and guard.** The only press-state child was `CheckCircle`; the kit now makes every small visual (`[data-pace-inner] > [data-pace-visual]`) transparent to the pointer, so the target is always the control; `state-layer.test.ts` pins the rule, journeys e2e presses the real check.

### 139. A kit default that silently beats the caller's class; a control with no width limit
- **Bug.** Four text-fit faults the route sweep and worst-case gallery found at 320–390 px: «ASAP» broken into «AS / AP» (the kit `Text` sets `flexShrink: 1` inline, which beat the caller's `shrink-0` class); a button label squeezed to a letter a line by its own Ctrl+Enter hint on a phone-width window; «Это не я — завершить сеанс» wider than the screen (a kit button is `shrink-0` with no `max-width`); a task row's meta line and tag overflowing their boxes (the tag in a column wrapper sized to its content, the meta line cut without its full value for the `title`). Also a desktop Week lane drawn 38 px wide inside a 44 px target, covering its neighbour.
- **Missed because.** Screen tasks ran jest only; jest has no layout, and the sweeps that catch these ran for the first time in CI at the end of the PR.
- **Class.** A kit component whose inline style or fixed minimum overrides what a caller asks for, without saying so; a control with a minimum size and no maximum.
- **Sweep and guard.** `Text` keeps a caller's `shrink-0`; `KeyHint` shows only on wide windows; `Button` is `max-w-full`; `Tag` is `max-w-full` and the meta line's tag never shrinks (half the line at most) while the meta text truncates with its full value; Week folds a run on a computer before a lane gets under 44 px (`lanesThatFit`, the grid measures its own width). Jest pins each rule (`text.test`, `key-hint.test`, `button.test`, `week-screen.test`); the route sweep and the worst-case gallery stay the layout guard, run on the commit that changes a kit component.

### 140. A request that ends something on the server, sent once with its failure swallowed
- **Bug.** Log out with no network signed the device out and left the session alive on the server: `auth.logout` sent `POST /api/auth/logout` once and dropped any failure (`catch {}`), so the token stayed valid until it expired and the session stayed in Devices with nothing saying so (QA round 22 R22-02, the server half; audit of PR #54).
- **Missed because.** The tests pinned "signed out locally even when the call fails" (the device half) and nothing asked what the server knew afterwards; e2e ran the log-out online only.
- **Class.** A write whose effect is a security or data promise to the person (ending a session, revoking access) made fire-and-forget: one attempt, the error read as "it will expire anyway".
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept the client's swallowed calls (`packages/client/src`): `notifications.read` is retried on the next open, `notifications.resolve` and `calendar.skip` report failure to their callers, `decisions.resolve` is the person's own record (not an end), the device list's revoke and `endThisDevice` rethrow. Only the log-out was a promise kept by no one. Now the ended token is kept (`EndedSessionStore`: the secure store / localStorage per server, in memory as the fallback), sent only to the log-out, again at the next start, back online, back in the app and after a login, and dropped once the server answers or says it no longer knows it (401, the contract the API pins: a repeated log-out answers 401, `auth.int.test.ts`). One request per token at a time; none after it settled. Settings › Devices says so while one waits. Guards: `auth-revoke.test.ts` (keep, retry, settle on 200 and 401, keep on 5xx, one request at once, next start, nothing else sent with the old token), `secure-session.test.ts`, `sync-frame.test.tsx` and `app-state.test.tsx` (the retry triggers), `sessions.test.tsx` (the line), `e2e/login.e2e.ts` (offline Sign out, back online, the server forgets the token, the network goes quiet).

### 141. An accessible name that says less than the row shows
- **Bug.** A Projects row was named by its `label` (the project's name) and a screen reader never heard "3 tasks" (audit 4.8); the Presets row dropped «Default»/«Yours» the same way; and the kit's `ListRow` with `href` did not pass `label` to its `Link` at all, so a link row could not be given a name that says more than its title (found building the More › Notifications row, R22-24).
- **Missed because.** Tests found rows by their name alone (`getByRole("link", { name: "Algebra" })`), which passed whether or not the count was read; nothing compared the name with what the row draws.
- **Class.** A `label`/`aria-label` override that stands in for a whole row but carries only part of its visible text; a kit wrapper that silently drops a prop on one of its branches.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept every `ListRow label=` (Projects, Presets, More) and the kit's frames: Projects and Presets now say the name and the trailing text, and `ListRow` passes `label` on its link branch too. Guards: `screens.test.tsx` and `presets.test.tsx` find the rows by the whole name, `list-row.test.tsx` pins a link row's label. Rule: a row's label repeats everything the row shows that matters (name, count, kind).

### 142. A layout fact guessed from a proxy instead of measured
- **Bug.** The Inbox offered «Show all» when a capture was longer than 160 characters, not when its four lines were cut: six short lines were clamped with no «Show all», and a 170-character text that fit got a redundant one (audit 4.7, F-DAT-06). Devices in Settings loaded under three task-row skeletons, 108 px taller than its one row, and the buttons under it jumped as it loaded (an e2e click missed until it waited for the list; QA 22 fix round 2).
- **Missed because.** jest has no layout, so the tests fed one long single-line text and one short one; the skeleton's default shape was never compared with the list it stood for.
- **Class.** Whether something overflows, or how tall it will be, decided from a stand-in (a character count, a default placeholder) where the layout itself can say.
- **Sweep and guard.** `ListRow` takes `onTitleClamped` (`ui/clamped-title.tsx`: the whole text laid out out of sight at the same width, the two heights compared, the same on a phone and the web); the length stays only until the first layout. Other `titleLines` users (task rows, presets) offer no «Show all» and need none. Skeletons swept: Devices now holds one device row (`shape="listRow" rows={1}`); the others already match their lists (`empty-state`, first sync). Guards: `inbox-screen.test.tsx` (clamped short text, unclamped long text, the length fallback), `sessions.test.tsx` (skeleton shape), the e2e no longer waits around the jump.

### 143. A chart drawn for a series of zeros
- **Bug.** Insights' «Focus by hour» drew 24 empty columns and no peak line for a week with rest and chores but no focus time (QA round 22 R22-18).
- **Missed because.** The insights tests seeded focus time or nothing at all (the whole screen's empty state); the in-between week was never drawn.
- **Class.** A chart whose emptiness is checked for the whole screen but not for its own series.
- **Sweep and guard.** Insights' other blocks already return nothing for an empty series (`fragmentationTiles`, `focusSleepRows`, the bars draw no fill at 0); the hours chart now says «No focus time this week.» in its card. Guard: `insights-screen.test.tsx` (a sleep-only week).

### 144. A destination reachable from one screen on one form factor only
- **Bug.** On a phone Notifications was reachable only from the bell in Now's header; More did not list it, so a sign-in notice was out of reach from every other screen (QA round 22 R22-24).
- **Missed because.** The More test counted the links it expected (five) instead of checking every top-level route against the phone's ways in.
- **Class.** A top-level route that the sidebar reaches on a computer but no tab or More row reaches on a phone.
- **Sweep and guard.** Swept `app/*.tsx` against the phone's tabs and More: Decisions is reached from Notifications, Presets from Settings, Review redirects to Inbox, task and project pages from their lists; Notifications now has a More row with the unread count. Guard: `more-screen.test.tsx` lists the six rows and the count.

### 145. Copy that names a choice after its own picker, and a verb pair split across two words
- **Bug.** The Rest picker's second item was «Отдых» / "Rest" again, «Дрёма» read archaic, «Закрыть как «Отменена»» read as a status (QA round 22 R22-22); EN signed in with "Sign in" and out with "Log out" (audit 4.13).
- **Missed because.** `glossary.test.ts` (class 134) pinned one verb for signing in but not its pair, and nothing compared a picker's items with its title.
- **Class.** Class 45 / 134 again: one action, two words; a list item that repeats its heading.
- **Sweep and guard.** EN uses Sign in / Sign out everywhere (Settings, the unsynced confirm, the server and OAuth lines, the bot's reply); the Rest item is «Безделье» / "Downtime", the nap «Дневной сон», the close button «Закрыть со статусом «…»». `glossary.test.ts` now rejects "log in/out" as a verb in EN, pins the pair, the Calendar hint word for word, and that no picker item equals the picker's name. e2e specs updated for every changed label.

### 146. An answer that claims to be "everything" listing its fields by hand
- **Bug.** Feedback 5 item 5: `get_task` ("everything about one task") copied nine fields of the projection by hand; `startAt`, `estimateMinutes`, the series, the sources and the status history were missing, and a client that reads only the text saw even less.
- **Missed because.** The test pinned the fields that were listed, not the projection; a field added to `Task` later had nothing to fail.
- **Class.** A full view of a model written as a hand-picked list: it drifts from the model silently with every new field.
- **Sweep and guard.** `get_task` now spreads the whole projection, and its output schema's extra fields are typed `satisfies Record<Exclude<keyof Task, …>, z.ZodType>`, so a new `Task` field fails the type check until it is answered; the text answer carries the same fields as JSON. Other answers (`list_now` rows, `fetch`) are summaries by design and say so. Follow-up (session review minor 8): the text answer no longer repeats the whole JSON: subtasks (with their numbers) and the closure (with its event and reason) are lines, the other fields follow as JSON with long text cut to 200 characters; `mcp-tools.int.test.ts` pins that a long description and source text are not repeated whole.

### 147. A height set on a padded field counts only its text
- **Bug.** Now's composer cut its placeholder («Добавьте задачу или текст») in half on every width (seen in the 2026-11-09 shots): the line set its height to one line of text, and the kit `TextField` input carries `py-2`, which a border-box textarea counts inside that height.
- **Missed because.** The composer test pinned the height to the line's type metrics only; no visual check of the empty composer after the kit moved the padding onto the input.
- **Class.** An explicit height or minimum computed from content metrics, set on an element whose padding is inside its box.
- **Sweep and guard.** The composer is the only caller that sets a field's height (`isGrowing` and `contentSize` searched); its empty and eight-line heights now add the input's padding, and `composer.test.tsx` reads both from the field's own classes.

### 148. Classes on a reanimated view, which the web drops
- **Bug.** Day's new row-aligned rail drew nothing visible on the web: each bar was an `Animated.View` with `className="absolute … tint"`, the classes never reached the element, and the bars stacked in the flow without colour (e2e `week.e2e.ts`, rail bars against their rows). The same mistake sat in `Disclosure` (`overflow-hidden` on its animated content) and the composer's unfold frame (its clip while growing): neither clipped on the web.
- **Missed because.** jest renders the className prop as given, so a test that reads classes passes; the skeleton had a comment about it, no rule did.
- **Class.** A NativeWind className on a component NativeWind does not interop (reanimated's `Animated.*`) on the web.
- **Sweep and guard.** Every `<Animated.* className>` in apps/app moved to style or a plain View inside (rail bars, Disclosure, composer unfold; the skeleton and update banner already did). An ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` rule now refuses `className` on any `Animated.*` element.

### 149. A correction that undoes more than the mark it names
- **Bug.** On a closed homework task the checklist stayed interactive: unticking a subtask revoked an old per-subtask `task.submitted` that named only it, and that event had often closed the task (`closes: true`), so one untick silently reopened the task (closure and outcome gone, back on Now) with no word; ticking a pending one on the same task was refused (`retro/task-closed`) (session review #3 of PR #54).
- **Missed because.** The untick tests ran on open tasks and on the fixture's non-closing old send; nobody asked what else the revoked event carried, and the two directions of the toggle were tested apart.
- **Class.** A correction (revoke/amend) chosen for one effect of an event that has others; a toggle whose two directions follow different rules.
- **Sweep and guard.** `unmarkDone` now refuses on a closed task (`retro/task-closed`, the same code as ticking), and the checklist is read-only (disabled) while the task is closed: reopen first. Other corrections that revoke for a side purpose: `undoneBy` was the only one (History's revoke names the whole event and asks first). Guards: `actions.test.ts` (an old closing per-subtask send, the untick refused, nothing written, the task stays closed), `task-screen.test.tsx` (a closed task's checkboxes are disabled; press and Space change nothing).

### 150. A behaviour retired in this build that older clients keep doing
- **Bug.** Feedback 5 dropped the automatic `cancelled_missed` close, but installed older builds still write it at a task's final deadline (`auto:<task>:missed`, `source: system`) and the server took it, so on a mixed fleet a task past its deadline was still closed automatically (session review minor 5 of PR #54).
- **Missed because.** The change was tested against this build's clients only; nothing asked what the server does with what an older build still sends.
- **Class.** A rule retired on the client side with no server-side answer for the clients that have not updated (class 13's forward-compat, the other direction: old writers, new reader).
- **Sweep and guard.** Refusing it would not do: an old client keeps a rejected event in its outbox and sends it again on every sync run (`sync-run.ts`: only `accepted` ids are marked synced), so the network would never go quiet. A client push now takes the miss and revokes it at once with a system `event.revoked` (`<miss id>:retired`, core `retiredAutoMissRevokes`), so the task stays open on every device, the old one included after its next pull; the old client does not write it again (its automatic outcomes skip ids already in the log, revoked ones included). Misses already in a log and anything written in-process stay as they are (the owner kept old closes). Other retired automatic writes: none (`skipped` is still made). Guards: `sync.int.test.ts` (taken, revoked, a retry writes nothing; in-process stays), `outcome.test.ts`. Release notes say it. Two known edges, accepted (code review of 293b78c..9039ae6, minors 5 and 6): an old client that confirmed the miss while offline ("confirm missed") sees its confirmation overridden once it syncs, since the revoke takes the close away and the task reopens; and an "ahead" instance an old client made when it closed the miss stays after the revoke, harmless, as its id is deterministic and this build's derivation finds it instead of writing a second one. The miss and its revoke are written in one write (class 170), and the revoke's id is core's `retiredId`, inside the class-12 id-length property test. The same direction for data rather than behaviour (a new key in a definition an old app parses strictly): class 188.

### 151. An address honoured on one form factor only
- **Bug.** `/?task=<id>` (feedback 5 item 6: the task open beside the list is in the address) opened the task only in the wide split; the same link opened in a phone-width browser showed the list and nothing else (session review minor 6 of PR #54).
- **Missed because.** The address tests mocked a wide window for the whole file; the web build at a phone's width is the same code with the other branch.
- **Class.** Class 144 again, for addresses: a param read by one layout's branch and ignored by the other.
- **Sweep and guard.** Now at a phone's width redirects a known `?task=` to `/task/<id>` (an unknown id stays on the list, nothing written: class 1). The other params of item 6 (`?day=`, `?week=`, History's filters) are read by screens that have one layout for both widths. Guard: `now-screen.test.tsx` (phone width: redirect; unknown id: none, nothing written).

### 152. A system setting each component asks for itself, answered after its first frame
- **Bug.** `useIsMotionReduced` started every component at `false` and asked the system in an effect, so a freshly mounted overlay, Presence, Disclosure or sheet played its first frames with full travel for a person who asked for reduced motion (UI review I1 of PR #54); each component also kept its own subscription.
- **Missed because.** The presence test waited for the answer before it rendered the frame it checked; nothing rendered an overlay's first frame with the setting on.
- **Class.** A process-wide fact (an accessibility setting, a media query) held as per-component state with an async first read: every mount starts wrong.
- **Sweep and guard.** One module store (`ui/use-reduced-motion.ts`): asked once as the app starts (`seedReducedMotion` from the root layout; the browser's media query is read at once), followed by one subscription, read with `useSyncExternalStore`. Other per-component system reads: the theme (`theme-provider`, one provider) and the window size (`useWindowDimensions`, synchronous) are already shared. Guards: `use-reduced-motion.test.ts` (right on the first render after seeding, one read and one subscription for three readers, follows a change), `presence.test.tsx` (the first drawn frame has no travel).

### 153. A role that promises a pattern its content does not follow
- **Bug.** The task's ⋯ menu said `role="menu"` twice (the wide popover's surface and the list inside it), its rows were buttons, and the delete confirm (a question with two buttons) sat inside the menu: a screen reader announced a menu whose items were not menuitems and whose arrow keys did nothing (UI review I2 of PR #54).
- **Missed because.** Tests found the rows by `button` and never asked what contained them; `getByRole("menu")` does not see a plain View that is not an accessibility element, so a role on a container was invisible to the queries.
- **Class.** An ARIA composite role (menu, listbox, grid) put on a container for its look, without the children's roles and the keyboard model it promises.
- **Sweep and guard.** The ⋯ menu is a dialog (the popover's default; the phone's sheet already was) holding buttons; it was the only `role="menu"` in the app. Guard: `task-screen.test.tsx` walks the host tree for any `menu` role at both widths, with the confirm open.

### 154. Idle machinery mounted once per item, and a page-wide read on every show
- **Bug.** Every web Tooltip kept its bubble mounted from the start, in a portal of its own in the page's body, laid out and measured while hidden (a Week of blocks: one portal per block); every show also measured every control on the page (`getBoundingClientRect` across the whole document) to keep the bubble off them, so moving along a row of icon buttons forced a full-page layout read on each hover. Escape closed the tooltip and the sheet under it at once (UI review I3 and a minor of PR #54).
- **Missed because.** The tests pinned what shows and when, on a screen with one or two triggers; nobody counted mounts or reads on a screen with hundreds.
- **Class.** Work sized by the page (a portal per item, a scan of the document) paid when nothing or one small thing changes; a key handled by two layers at once.
- **Sweep and guard.** The bubble mounts on the first show and unmounts on hide; the trigger's `aria-describedby` points at a `display: none` description beside it (always there, never laid out). The control scan (`tooltip-controls.ts`) reads the trigger's dialog when it is in one, measures at most 120 controls and is reused by a warm hover; a scroll or a resize forgets it. Escape stops at the tooltip while one shows. Other per-item portals: none (`Popover`/`Sheet` mount through `OverlayPresence` only while open). Guards: `tooltip.web.test.tsx` (no bubble until shown, gone after, the description there all along, Escape stops), `tooltip-controls.test.ts` (reuse, forget, another root, the cap).

### 155. One pane of a kind given the keyboard, its siblings left out
- **Bug.** Day's activity pane closed on Escape; the Inbox and Week inspector panes did not, and none of them moved the focus in when they opened or back to the row when they closed: a keyboard user picked a row, and the details appeared at the far edge with the focus still on the list (UI review I4 of PR #54).
- **Missed because.** Each pane was built in its feature and tested there; the kit's `Inspector` held only the frame, so nothing made a pane's keyboard behaviour common.
- **Class.** Behaviour that belongs to a kind of region (a pane, a sheet, a popover) added in one instance's feature instead of in the kit frame they share.
- **Sweep and guard.** The kit's `Inspector` now draws every pane in `InspectorFrame`: Escape calls the screen's `onInspectorClose` (Inbox, Week, Day; Day's editor keeps its own layer), focus moves into the pane as it opens (unless a field in it took it) and back to the latest opener as it closes (`platform/pane-focus.web.ts`; on Android the screen reader's focus moves in). Now's task pane was left without Escape: its inline editors did not yet own the key (closed by class 194). Sheets and popovers already had both in the kit. Guards: `inspector-pane.test.tsx`, `pane-focus.web.test.ts`, `inbox-wide.test.tsx` and `week-wide.test.tsx` (Escape closes).

### 156. A transition that starts from a remembered state instead of the current one
- **Bug.** Four kit minors of the UI review of PR #54: a reopened Popover showed for a frame where its anchor had been at the last opening (the box was measured on open and never forgotten); a Disclosure closed while still growing jumped to its full height before it shrank; the wide ⋯ menu's popover was unmounted the moment it closed, so its exit never played; and a TextField marked `isInvalid` drew the danger border but told a screen reader nothing (`aria-invalid` followed only the shown message).
- **Missed because.** The tests checked the settled states (open, closed, the error shown), not the first frame of a transition started from another transition, nor the attribute beside the colour.
- **Class.** The start of a transition (where an overlay appears, the height a close starts from, whether the overlay is still there to animate) read from what was true at an earlier step; a state said in one channel (colour) and not the other (ARIA).
- **Sweep and guard.** Popover forgets its anchor box as it opens again (kept through the exit); Disclosure's close starts from the height reached (`shrinkFrom`); the ⋯ menu's Popover stays mounted and is told `open={isOpen}`; `aria-invalid` is `isShown || isInvalid`. Swept the other overlays: Sheet and the other Popover callers already pass `open` and stay mounted; no other kit field draws the danger look without saying so. Guards: `popover.test.tsx` (a reopen waits for the fresh measure), `disclosure.test.tsx`, `task-screen.test.tsx` (the popover is told to close, not unmounted), `text-field.test.tsx`.

### 157. "In sight" read from the window, not from what the scroll view shows
- **Bug.** `e2e/targets.e2e.ts` failed at 390 on `/presets/hw` with «Переопределить: Прогресс» under the 44 px target. Every override row draws the same kit `Switch` (a 44 px target around its 32 px track), and only this one failed: once the «Сдача по подзадачам» row above it went, the Progress switch sat in the strip between its scroll view's visible bottom and the window's bottom, clipped behind the phone's tab bar, where the check's press 21 px below its middle landed on the bar. The check counted a control as in sight by its box against the window alone.
- **Missed because.** The check passed while no control happened to sit in that strip; removing an unrelated row moved one there.
- **Class.** A layout check whose "visible" is the window's rectangle, not what the person can see (a scroll view clips, an in-flow bar sits under it); a measure that is then true or false by scroll position.
- **Sweep and guard.** The control was not changed: its target is the kit's. The check now counts a control as in sight only when its top, middle and bottom land on it (not clipped, not covered), and scrolls the preset page's Work switches to the middle first, so the switch that failed is measured whole. Swept the specs for other window-only visibility checks (`innerHeight`): none.

### 158. A handler that reads the state it just asked for, before React draws it
- **Bug.** The e2e sweep in `overlays.e2e.ts` (hover each icon button, move away) left the Day's «Следующий день» tooltip on screen for good. That trigger shows its tooltip at once (`isImmediate`, a reason it is unavailable); the pointer left before React drew the shown state, so `leave` read `shown === null` from its closure, cancelled nothing and scheduled no hide. Nothing hid it after.
- **Missed because.** The unit tests fire enter and leave through `fireEvent`, each in its own `act`, so a render always ran between them; the e2e sweep used to give each tooltip time. The lazy-mount change (UI review I3) made the sweep wait for the bubble to go, which exposed it.
- **Class.** An event handler deciding from render state (a closure's `useState` value) while an update it set itself is still pending: two events in one frame see the old state.
- **Sweep and guard.** The tooltip keeps what it last asked for in a ref (`modeRef`) that `hover`, `keep` and `leave` read. Swept the kit's other pointer/timer handlers (Popover, Pressable state layer, Disclosure): they read refs or the event, not a just-set state. Guard: a unit test fires enter and leave in one `act` on an immediate tooltip; the e2e sweep now waits for each bubble to leave before the next hover.

### 159. A layout decision taken from the window where the region decides
- **Bug.** In the 400 px task pane beside Now (desktop), the footer laid Focus, Done with its «Ctrl+Enter» hint and «Другое время…» on one row: the window was wide, so the hints showed and the third button took words, and «Готово» stood one letter a line, «Фо/ку/с» beside it (design review B1, `states/now-task-pane--desktop-*`). Class 139 had already fixed the same squeeze on a phone-width *window*.
- **Missed because.** `KeyHint` and the footer asked `useIsWide()` (the window), jest has no layout, and the pane at its narrowest was in no gallery or sweep: the full task page at 720 has room for all three.
- **Class.** A width-dependent choice (show a hint, a label, a third control) made from the window's breakpoint for content that lives in a narrower region (the inspector, a popover, a split column).
- **Sweep and guard.** `KeyHintRoomContext` lets a region say it has no room for shortcut hints; the task footer says so in the pane, keeps two equal halves on its row and moves «Другое время или итог…» under them in words (the same on a phone, where it was a bare glyph, review M15). Swept the other `useIsWide` users rendered inside the pane (task header, description, subtasks, the composer form's «Создать»): each keeps one control per line or hugs its label. Guards: `task-footer-pane.test.tsx` (two buttons on the row, no hint, the other time under them), the worst-case gallery draws the footer at the inspector's 360 minimum.

### 160. `flex-1` in a column wrapper, which zeroes the height it was given
- **Bug.** The time bar's tiles were 44 px pills on the web, though the class said `h-16`: each tile was `flex-1` inside the tooltip's own `flex-1` column wrapper, so its flex-basis was 0 along the column, which beats `height` in CSS; only the target's 44 px minimum held it up (design review H5). The Calendar tile with nothing near was also dashed and greyed, the draft language, so it read as disabled (F-COL-12).
- **Missed because.** jest reads the class names as given; the shots showed the tiles small and nobody measured them against the spec's 56.
- **Class.** A `flex-1` meant for the row's main axis, put on a child whose parent is a column (a wrapper the kit adds), where it sizes the other axis.
- **Sweep and guard.** The tile is `w-full` with its own `h-bar-tile` token (56) inside the tooltip's wrapper, `fill.2` at rest, never dashed; running, the cell with a stroke bar (the stroke on `fill.3` measured under 3:1, so the pair is `bg.cell`, already in `pairs.ts`). Swept the other `Tooltip isFilling` children (Done in the task footer: a `Button` whose height is its own min-height, not a fixed height, so it is unaffected). Guard: `bar-tiles.test.tsx` refuses `flex-1` on a tile and pins the height token, `fill.2` and no dashed edge.

### 161. A fixed region that scrolls inside its budget instead of folding
- **Bug.** The time bar met the 40 % region budget by scrolling its cards and proposal inside a `ScrollView` of `0.4 × window − tiles`: on a phone with two running activities the second full card was cut in half under the tiles, and a typed line's proposal («Ассистент запишет …» with Confirm) sat scrolled under the tile row, so the person could not see what they were confirming (design review B2, `routes/now--phone-*`, `states/time-typed-*`). With the nav bar the chrome still took 47 % of the window.
- **Missed because.** The budget was "enforced" by a max height, which a test reads as satisfied; nothing measured the chrome against the window with two activities, and the spec's fold steps were never built.
- **Class.** A fixed region (a bar, a footer, a header) kept within a size limit by scrolling its own content, so content is covered rather than rearranged; a budget stated as a max height instead of steps.
- **Sweep and guard.** The bar no longer scrolls: what runs alongside is a compact row in the one running card (fold step 1), the typed field folds under «Начать другое» once the measured bar plus the nav bar pass 40 % and the bar has been left alone (step 2; a field the person unfolded stays), and on a phone the typed line's proposal opens as a sheet (`time-proposal`); a computer's column ≥ 680 lays the card beside the field and the tiles. Swept the other fixed regions (`Screen`'s footer is only the time bar; sheets and the composer scroll with the page). Guards: `time-bar.test.tsx` (one card with an alongside row, no scroll inside the bar, the budget fold and the hand-opened exception, the proposal in a sheet), `e2e/time.e2e.ts` (bar + nav ≤ 40 % of the window with two running, phone and desktop), `e2e/sheet-focus.e2e.ts` (the new sheet's keyboard).

### 162. A column centred in its region where the layout anchors it
- **Bug.** On a computer, Week, Inbox, Settings, Projects, Insights, History and Notifications put their large title at x = 396, Now and Day at 280, and the task page at 380: `Screen` centred its 960 column in the content region unless a screen opted into `anchor`, and only Now and Day did; the task title kept the phone's 16 gutter under a 32 header (design review H1).
- **Missed because.** `e2e/layout.e2e.ts` checked that every title sits at one *y* (F-LAY-02) and never the x; the opt-in default hid the rule from every new screen.
- **Class.** A shared layout rule that each screen must opt into (an anchor, a gutter), so the default is the exception the spec forbids.
- **Sweep and guard.** `Screen` anchors every column at the region's leading edge on a computer (`wide` 960 by default, `content` 720 for Now); the task page's title takes the header's desktop gutter outside the pane. Swept the other centred columns (`composer-pane` is the inspector's own; `sync-frame` and `incompatible-screen` are full-window messages, centred by design). Guards: `screen-column.test.tsx` (no anchor still starts at the edge), `e2e/layout.e2e.ts` (every destination's title and the task page's at one x at 1440).

### 163. A slot sized for English where Russian cuts the part that carries the meaning
- **Bug.** In Russian the estimate chips read «По умолчанию (3…» (the number, the only information, cut by the 180 px chip cap), the edit sheet's start «Начало 5 нояб. 1…», the deadline type «Основной и окончате…», preset names «Домаш…» beside their kind and the reorder arrows, «Начать с «Отложен…»», the composer's placeholder «Добавьте задачу или», «Как в системе» broke inside its segment and «Очистить — подтвердить в Telegram» ran three lines (design review H3). English fit every slot.
- **Missed because.** The worst-case gallery feeds long *user* content; UI strings and formatted values in Russian were only seen in the shots, and the chip's cap was written for names, then reused for values.
- **Class.** A fixed or capped slot (chip cap, segment, a row's title beside trailing controls, a field's placeholder) sized by the English string, and a value formatted into words where the number comes last.
- **Sweep and guard.** Values keep their number: the estimate chip shows «~30 мин» and says «По умолчанию (30 мин)» in its name; date and estimate chips and «Начать с …» take their row (`Chip isWhole`), names keep the cap. Short RU copy where a slot is fixed: «Задача или текст», «Два срока», «Системная», «Очистить через Telegram» / «Удалить через Telegram». Layout where the label is long: a preset's kind goes under its name, the account confirmations stack on a phone (`SheetActions isStacked`). Guards: `chip.test.tsx` (a whole chip has no cap), `composer.test.tsx` (the default estimate shows its number), `presets.test.tsx` (the kind in the title's column), `sheet-actions.test.tsx`; the RU phone shots stay the layout check.

### 164. Two clocks read apart, and a mark whose only contrast is a tint
- **Bug.** Day's red now line was missing on the phone and in dark on the desktop: the line looks for the row whose time holds now, a running row's end is the day model's "now", and the screen's clock (`useClock`) and the model's recompute tick on separate 30 s timers, so whenever the screen's now ran ahead of the model's the running row no longer "held" it and the line vanished. Where it showed, it sat at the main row's bottom while the alongside running bar ran 50 px past it, and its time ran into the card's edge. The rail bars were tints at 1.15:1 (light) and 1.32:1 (dark) on the page with a 1 px cap as the only stroke (design review H4).
- **Missed because.** Tests built the rail and the clock from one instant; the shots happened to catch the two clocks in step on one viewport only. The bar pairs were never in `pairs.ts`: a fill read as a "decoration".
- **Class.** A check that compares a stored "now" (inside a model) with a live one read elsewhere; a mark that carries meaning drawn only as a tint.
- **Sweep and guard.** `dayRail` lets a running row hold now from its start, whatever its stored end, and puts the line in the last row that holds now; every running bar reaches down to it; the time sits above the line inside the card. Each bar is edged all round in its stroke (`border-stroke`) (`hue.*.stroke` on `bg.app`, ≥ 3:1, in `pairs.ts` with the reason). Swept the other "end = now" readers: the week grid and Insights take the model's own `now`, not a second clock. Guards: `day-rail.test.ts` (a running row a tick behind the clock, the last running row), `day-rail.test.tsx` (the main bar reaches the line, the edge classes, the time's place), `e2e/time.e2e.ts` (the line on Day at 390 and 1440, its time inside the timeline).

### 165. A platform's default line breaking, which splits a word to make it fit
- **Bug.** Week on a phone showed «Музы|ка» and «Пошё|л в ЦСС» in its narrow lanes: react-native-web's Text breaks a word that does not fit its line (`overflow-wrap: break-word`), so a clamped label wrapped inside words (design review H6).
- **Missed because.** jest has no line breaking; the worst-case gallery fed long names into wide boxes, where every word fits.
- **Class.** Text in a box narrower than its longest word, left to the platform's break rules instead of a stated policy (wrap between words, or one cut line).
- **Sweep and guard.** A Week box measures its width and, when its longest word cannot fit, shows one line cut with an ellipsis (`boxLabelFit`); otherwise it wraps between words over the lines its height holds, and on the web words never break inside themselves. The whole name stays in the tooltip, the box's accessible name and the details. Swept the other narrow clamped labels (Day's rail has none; Insights bars truncate on one line; task rows wrap at 390 where words fit). Guard: `box-label.test.ts`.

### 166. A control that appears beside a label and pushes the row past the screen
- **Bug.** On a past day at 390, Day's period row (‹ «воскресенье · 8 нояб.» › and «Сегодня») ran to x = 390 and cut «Сегодня» at the screen's edge (design review H7): the short label existed for 320 windows only, and the group did not shrink inside the header row.
- **Missed because.** The stepper was tried on today (no reset shown) and at 320 (the short label); the away state at 390 in Russian was only in a shot.
- **Class.** A slot kept for a control that shows in one state (the reset away from today), sized for the state without it. Class 163's kind, seen through a state rather than a language.
- **Sweep and guard.** `PeriodStepper` shows the short label on a phone's window (≤ 430) while the reset shows, and the group shrinks within its row; Day now passes a short label («вс, 8 нояб.»), as History already did; Week's range label is short already. Guard: `period-stepper.test.tsx` (short on a 390 phone away, long on today and on a computer).

- **Follow-up (the sweep still failed in the full e2e run).** A second path left a bubble up: until its first layout a bubble waited at the window's corner (0, 0), unseen (opacity 0) but taking the pointer. The sweep moves the pointer to (0, 0) after each hover; under a loaded run the bubble was not measured yet, so the pointer landed on it: the trigger's leave scheduled the hide and the bubble's enter (`keep`) called it off. Once measured the bubble moved next to its trigger, away from the resting pointer, and no event ever told it the pointer was gone. The same class: a decision from a state that is not yet what the page shows (an unplaced bubble treated as a placed one). Fix: an unplaced bubble is `visibility: hidden`, `pointer-events: none` and has no pointer handlers; and while a hover tooltip shows, a `pointermove` anywhere off its trigger and its bubble starts the hide once (`usePointerAway`; repeated moves do not postpone it), so no missed leave can keep one up. Guards: `tooltip.web.test.tsx` (an unplaced bubble takes no pointer and goes after the grace; a move elsewhere hides a hover tooltip, not a keyboard one). Not reproduced locally (the e2e passes in isolation against the shared servers, six runs); found from the code and the run's timing.

### 167. A key stopped in one listener while other layers hear it on another listener or event
- **Bug.** A keyboard-focused trigger in a pane or a sheet showed its tooltip, and Escape closed the tooltip *and* the pane or the sheet (code review of the fix wave 293b78c..9039ae6, I1). The tooltip stopped the keydown's propagation from its own window capture listener, but the shortcut registry (`use-hotkey.web.tsx`) listens on the same window in the same phase and had registered first (InspectorFrame's, SheetActions' Cancel, the composer form's, Day's activity pane's Escape all fired), and react-native-web's `Modal` (Sheet, Dialog, Popover) closes on a document **keyup** Escape, which nothing stopped. The same keyup closed a sheet around a field that handles Escape itself (a subtask being typed in the edit sheet): the field closed its entry on the keydown, and the sheet went on the keyup.
- **Missed because.** The unit test passed a mocked `stopPropagation` and asserted it was called; nothing dispatched a real event with another listener on the same target or a keyup after it, and no e2e pressed Escape at a tooltip inside a container.
- **Class.** Two layers deciding about the same key independently: `stopPropagation` (which does not stop other listeners on the same target) relied on against a listener registered earlier, and a key held back on keydown while another layer acts on its keyup.
- **Sweep and guard.** One arbiter for Escape on the web (`ui/escape-first.ts`): a shown tooltip pushes itself as an Escape taker; the shortcut registry asks it first, and its own window capture listener covers the case where no shortcut is mounted; a taken Escape stops every other listener on the window (`stopImmediatePropagation`) and its keyup is swallowed on the window before the document hears it. A field that owns Escape (`data-pace-keys`) gets its keyup swallowed the same way. Swept the app's other `stopPropagation`s (`deadline-policy-field`, `task-fields`: touch starts, not keys; `text-link.web`: a click) and Escape handlers (`subtask-list`, `quick-line`: covered by `data-pace-keys`). Guards: `escape-first.test.tsx` (jsdom, real events: the registry's Escape shortcut and a document keyup listener standing in for the Modal stay silent at a tooltip and hear the next Escape; a field's Escape keeps its sheet), `tooltip.web.test.tsx` (a real Escape on the window, nothing mocked), `e2e/tooltips.e2e.ts` (a tooltip on a sheet's cross and on an inspector pane's cross at 1280×900: the first Escape takes the tooltip, the second the container).

- **Follow-up (full e2e on 44c4f5f).** Two regressions of this fix: (1) the keyup swallow also took fields marked `own` (they keep their typing keys, not Escape), so Escape in Day's «С» field no longer closed the sheet; only `escape` fields hold the keyup now (test: an `own` field lets Escape close its sheet). (2) A hover tooltip in its grace after a click took the next Escape, meant for the sheet the click opened; any press now hides a tooltip at once (`tooltip-dismiss.ts`, test in `tooltip.web.test.tsx`). Missed because the unit tests covered only the cases the change was for; the Day and time e2e caught both.

### 168. A cap applied before the filter that decides what matters
- **Bug.** The tooltip's control scan (`tooltip-controls.ts`) measured the first 120 controls in DOM order and only then kept those near the trigger. On Week (hundreds of blocks) a trigger late in the DOM lost its neighbours, and its bubble could cover a neighbouring block (bug class 6; code review of 293b78c..9039ae6, I2).
- **Missed because.** The cap test only counted measurements; no test put the trigger's neighbour past the cap.
- **Class.** A bound on work (a cap, a page, a sample) taken over the whole input in an arbitrary order, before the filter that says which items matter: the bound drops the items it was meant to keep.
- **Sweep and guard.** The scan now measures every visible control once (one layout, then reads; reused by warm hovers) and caps after the nearness filter, keeping the nearest. Other caps before a filter: `activity-task-field` (filters, then slices), `drafts.ts` and the usage lists (sorted, then sliced) are in the right order. Guard: `tooltip-controls.test.ts` (a neighbour after 240 far controls is found; the cap keeps the nearest).

### 169. A cache's age refreshed on every reuse
- **Bug.** The tooltip's control scan set its timestamp on every reuse, so a chain of warm hovers each within 600 ms of the last kept one scan alive indefinitely, though the page under it could have changed (a list re-rendered, a pane opened) without a scroll or a resize to forget it (code review of 293b78c..9039ae6, minor 7).
- **Missed because.** The reuse test checked that a reuse measures nothing, with a reuse that happened to slide the window; nothing asked how old the reused data was.
- **Class.** A freshness window measured from the last read instead of from when the data was taken (sliding expiry where a fixed one was meant).
- **Sweep and guard.** The timestamp is set only when the scan is taken. Other in-app caches with a reuse window: none slide (the store's caches are invalidated by events, not by time). Guard: `tooltip-controls.test.ts` (a chain of reuses 400 ms apart measures again once the scan is older than the window).

### 170. One fact written in two writes, and a server event dropped when it fails to parse
- **Bug.** An old client's pushed automatic miss and the server's `:retired` revoke of it went in as two writes with an announcement between them: another device could pull (or be sent over its live socket) the miss without its revoke, and a crash between the two left the miss for good, since a retried push finds the miss known and writes no revoke. And `#system` dropped a server event that failed to parse without a word (code review of 293b78c..9039ae6, minor 3).
- **Missed because.** The sync test pulled after the push had finished, when both writes were in; nothing watched what another device heard in between, nor what a parse failure did.
- **Class.** A rule's consequence written after the event it answers, in its own write, where both must be seen together or not at all; a server-built value that fails its own schema treated as nothing to do.
- **Sweep and guard.** The revokes are computed from the screened batch (ids not yet in the state) and inserted with it in one write (`system-events.ts` `withRetiredMisses`); `stampSystem` refuses a system event that does not parse, for the derive steps too (reported and left out since class 184, not thrown). Other answers written in a later write: the derive steps (instances, outcomes) are separate on purpose (they follow from the state, and a retry derives them again). Guard: `live.int.test.ts` (another device's socket hears the miss and its revoke in one message).

### 171. A Back that assumes there is somewhere to go back to
- **Bug.** The task screen's Back called `router.back()` outright. Now's `?task=` link opened at a phone's width hands over to `/task/<id>` with a `Redirect`, which replaces the history entry, and a task link opened straight on the web has no entry before it either: Back did nothing (code review of 293b78c..9039ae6, minor 8). Deleting the task from its menu, which leaves through the same back, stayed on a screen whose task was gone.
- **Missed because.** The test router's `canGoBack` answers true; no test opened the task with nothing behind it.
- **Class.** A navigation step that relies on history the route may not have (a deep link, a replace); class 151's other face (an address honoured one way only).
- **Sweep and guard.** `useTaskBack` goes back when it can and to Now otherwise, as `useBack` and the preset screen already did; those were the only other `router.back` callers. Guard: `task-screen.test.tsx` (with nothing to go back to, Back replaces to `/`).

### 172. Text cut by UTF-16 code units, splitting a character
- **Bug.** MCP `get_task`'s text answer cut each long string with `slice(0, 200)`, which can stop between the two halves of an emoji and leave a lone surrogate in the answer; the closure's reason was not cut at all (code review of 293b78c..9039ae6, minor 9). The same cut stood in core's `fitText` (a task's title, subtask labels, link and description written by actions) and the quick line's title cap (`parse-quick-input.ts`), both writing user content into events.
- **Missed because.** The tests cut ASCII and Cyrillic (one unit per character); none put an emoji at the cut.
- **Class.** A length limit applied with `String.prototype.slice` on user text: code units are not characters (class of `clipText`, which already counted graphemes for display).
- **Sweep and guard.** `get_task` cuts with core's `clipText` (graphemes) and cuts the reason too; `fitText` and the title cap use the new `sliceWhole` (the longest start within a code-unit limit that ends between graphemes, so stored fields still fit their schema's length). Left as they are, ASCII by construction: `middleEllipsis` (hosts, punycode), the user agent, the crash context's error text. Not swept further: `task-summary.tsx`'s link head/tail (a display cut of a URL) and `auth-routes.ts`'s place parts (from the edge's geo data), noted for a later pass. Guards: `limits.test.ts` (property: never ill-formed, within the limit), `parse-quick-input.test.ts`, `mcp-tools.int.test.ts` (an emoji at the cut, the reason cut).

### 173. A live number beside a name in a row that also holds a control
- **Bug.** The B2 running card put the large elapsed time on the name's line, before Stop: in Russian at 390 the main row read «Пош… 2 ч 02 мин [Стоп]» and the alongside row «N 1 ч 32 мин параллельно [Стоп]», a one-letter name. The folded bar with two activities also took 386 of 844 px with the nav bar, over the 40 % budget the e2e asserts (QA of the merged design fixes). The `time-proposal` sheet's e2e opener checked for the fold row right after a navigation, found nothing yet, and waited for a field that stayed folded.
- **Missed because.** The jest tests used a short English name and "5m"; the budget was estimated, never measured before the e2e ran; the opener's check had no wait.
- **Class.** A row where a name shares the width with a number that grows (a live time) and a control, so the name, the part that says what it is, is the one that yields; an e2e check of "is it there?" made before the page has drawn it.
- **Sweep and guard.** The time sits under the name with the Expect, the alongside row drops «параллельно» (its accessible name keeps it) and stops with an icon button named «Стоп: …», and the card's padding tightens so the bar fits the budget (docs/design.md, 2026-11-10). Swept other rows with a trailing live number: Day's rows put the duration in their own trailing column with the name wrapping; task rows truncate the meta line, not the title. The opener and `time.e2e.ts` share `e2e/support/time-bar.ts`, which waits for the field or the fold row before deciding. Guards: `running-card.test.tsx` (a long Russian name with «2 ч 02 мин»: the time is not on the name's line; the alongside row has no «параллельно» and an icon Stop), `e2e/time.e2e.ts` (the budget), `e2e/sheet-focus.e2e.ts`.

### 174. A width guessed from a letter count, and a fixed-width demo in a narrower window
- **Bug.** The worst-case sweep found Week boxes 56 px wide whose name stuck out by 2–5 px («Домашка по алгебре 6», "Algebra homework 6"): `boxLabelFit` guessed 7 px a caption letter, «Домашка» is 58 px, so the box wrapped a word that could not fit and, with words no longer broken, it overflowed. The gallery's task footer at the inspector's 360 minimum was 380 wide in a 320 window and scrolled the page sideways. `week.e2e.ts` found the box's tooltip text twice once the box kept the whole name in its own text.
- **Missed because.** The H6 fix had a unit test with round numbers, not a measured Cyrillic word; the gallery entry was tried at 390 and 1440 only.
- **Class.** Class 151's proxy (a guessed size where the layout could say) on the safe side too thin; a demo frame with a fixed width that ignores the window.
- **Sweep and guard.** The letter width is 9 px (on the wide side: a wrong guess only shows the ellipsis sooner); the gallery footer is `w-full max-w-inspector-min`. The Week e2e scopes the tooltip check to the bubble and asserts the narrow box's label is cut. Guards: `box-label.test.ts` (the 58-in-56 case), `e2e/worst-case.e2e.ts` at 320.

### 175. A control drawn in a second, home-made look where the kit already had the agreed one
- **Bug.** The running card's alongside row stopped its activity with an `IconButton` holding an *outlined* 18 px square and nothing under it: at a row's end it read as an unchecked checkbox («отметить Музыку»), on the same card as the main Stop's filled square on `fill.2` (design review 2 N1). The row had no `HueTile` either, though a running activity in a row leads with one (owner 2026-11-08).
- **Missed because.** The B2 test asked only that the Stop was an icon with the right name; nothing asked what the glyph looked like, and F-CMP-04 (never an outlined square) lived in a comment on the picker's Stop, not in a check.
