- **Class.** One action drawn two ways on one surface, the second built from parts instead of the kit's look for it, so a rule that the first look already obeyed (filled, tonal) is lost.
- **Sweep and guard.** `IconButton` takes `tonal` (a 32 `fill.2` disc in the target) and `isFilled`, and the alongside Stop uses both behind a `HueTile`. Other squares: the picker's Stop and the task footer's Focus are filled. Guards: `running-card.test.tsx` (the alongside row has a tile, a `fill.2` disc and a filled glyph), `ui/stop-glyph-callers.test.ts` (every `icon="square"` in the app is filled).

### 176. One value formatted by two hand-rolled formatters, and a control's label left free to wrap
- **Bug.** Week named its range with its own `monthDay – monthDay` («9 нояб. – 15 нояб.»), Insights with core's `formatWeekRange` («9 – 15 нояб.»): the same week read two ways, and Week's longer form wrapped its month under the days inside the period pill at 390 in Russian (design review 2 N2).
- **Missed because.** Week's tests read the English range only, and nothing said the period label stays on one line; the wrap shows only with Russian month abbreviations at a phone's width.
- **Class.** A display format re-derived in a screen where a shared formatter exists (the two drift); a control's label with the default `wrap` policy (class 173's sibling: the text that should yield is the one the layout never told to).
- **Sweep and guard.** Week uses `formatWeekRange` (cross-month «28 сент. – 4 окт.»), and `PeriodStepper`'s label is one line (`truncate`) for Day, Week, History and Insights. Other date ranges built by hand: Day's and the picked block's are clock ranges with their own job, left. Guard: `week-screen.test.tsx` (RU, within a month and across two, one line).

### 177. A budget asserted only in the state the test happened to reach, with the common state exempted
- **Bug.** The 40 % time-bar budget was asserted with two activities freshly started; the state real use and every matrix shot are in (the first past twice its Expect, asking «Ещё занимаетесь?») added a ~40 px row of its own and an exemption in docs/design.md, so default Now on a phone took 377 of 844 px (44.7 %). The fold row «Начать другое» spent another ~34 px on gaps and was indented to a list row's inset (x = 44) beside a card and tiles at 12 (design review 2 N3).
- **Missed because.** The e2e started both activities a moment before measuring, so nothing ran past 2×; the jest test only checked that the question is absent within 2×.
- **Class.** A limit checked in the state that is easy to set up rather than the one people live in; a state-dependent extra row exempted from a budget instead of laid into the rows already there.
- **Sweep and guard.** The question takes the time's line and «Да, ещё» stands before Stop (no row of its own); the fold row uses `Disclosure inset="edge"` with no bar gaps around it. Other states of the bar that add height: the proposal opens as a sheet on a phone (B2), the field folds. Guards: `running-card.test.tsx` (the question shares the name's column, "Yes, still" shares Stop's row), `time-bar.test.tsx` (the fold row is on the edge, no bar gap), `e2e/time.e2e.ts` (the budget with the first activity 122 min into a 20 min Expect).

### 178. A hover affordance gated on the event's pointer type instead of the device's ability to hover
- **Bug.** On a phone-width touch browser a tooltip came up after a tap and stayed over the header («Предыдущий день» over «День», «Во входящие» over the header): the tooltip skipped only `pointerType === "touch"`, and a tap's compatibility events can arrive as a mouse's, after which nothing hovers away. On every size a header control's bubble went above it, over the large title (design review 2 N4).
- **Missed because.** The unit test fired a "touch" pointer only; the placement tests put triggers mid-window, never in the header band.
- **Class.** Device capability inferred from one event's self-description (pointer type) where the platform says it directly (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`, which the hotkey hints already read); a placement preference that ignores what lies on the preferred side by design (the title above a header).
- **Sweep and guard.** The tooltip shows on hover only when the media query matches (read when the pointer comes); a trigger whose top is within 120 px of the window prefers below. Other hover-only affordances: the Pressable's hover layer is CSS under `@media (hover: hover)` already; the key hints use the same media query. Guards: `tooltip.web.test.tsx` (a mouse-type enter on a coarse device shows nothing), `tooltip-place.test.ts` (a header trigger goes below, above when below is taken).

### 179. A control that appears with a state and takes its width from the content beside it
- **Bug.** On a computer the composer's + is offered only once the line holds text (F-CMP-18); it came with no slot kept for it, so the field jumped from x = 264 to 306 the moment the first letter was typed (design review 2 N5).
- **Missed because.** The tests asked whether + is there in each state, never where the field stands across them.
- **Class.** A conditionally rendered control in a row whose siblings flex: showing it shifts the content the person is working in (a layout shift on a state change).
- **Sweep and guard.** The line keeps a hidden slot of the target's width while + is away. Other conditional controls in rows: the period stepper keeps its reset's slot and its › hidden (not unmounted); the toast, the inspector's actions and the running card's state swap within the same box. Guard: `composer.test.tsx` (at 1440 the slot is there, hidden, while empty, and gives way to + with text).

### 180. A state layer drawn on the press area where the eye sees the row as the object
- **Bug.** On a computer the open task's row in Now's list looked half highlighted: the selection's `fill.1` lies under the whole row, but the pointer still resting on the row after the click drew the press area's hover overlay from the title (x = 312), so the check circle sat on a lighter strip beside a grey slab (design review 2 N7). Every task row hovered the same half way.
- **Missed because.** The selection test checked the fill on the row's box (it is there); the hover overlay is CSS on the press area, which no test related to the row.
- **Class.** A row built of two controls side by side (a check, then a press area) whose state layers belong to the parts, while the row reads as one object; the shot's state (selected and hovered) combined two layers nobody looked at together.
- **Sweep and guard.** `ControlRow` marks its box `data-pace-row`; on a hovering device the press area's own hover overlay is off and the row's whole box takes it. Other split rows: Day's rows and the settings rows are one Pressable each; the subtask checklist's rows put the check inside the row's press. Guards: `state-layer.test.ts` (the row rule, and the press area's own off inside it), `task-row.test.tsx` (the mark).

### 181. A one-line cut that takes the end of the line, where the end holds the value that matters
- **Bug.** A task row's meta line on a phone read «Срок завтра 19:30 · осталось 2…», «осталос…», "23h 59m l…": the line is cut at its end and the countdown, the piece the row is read for, came after the absolute date (design review 2 N10, H3's class).
- **Missed because.** The meta tests read the line's words in a wide test window's order with nothing cut; the cut only shows at 390 with real dates, and the gallery's rows were not read for which piece the cut took.
- **Class.** A truncation policy (cut at the end) applied to pieces in reading order without asking which piece should yield (class 173's sibling: the part that yields must be the least important one).
- **Sweep and guard.** On a phone `MetaLine` puts the due date after the other pieces, so the date is what the cut takes; a wide line keeps the date first. Other cut lines with pieces: the running card's Expect (one piece), Day's meta (time range first, the name wraps). Guards: `task-row.test.tsx` (phone: countdown before the date; computer: date first), `now-screen.test.tsx`.

### 182. A shortcut hint shown by the window's width in a header that lives in a narrow pane
- **Bug.** The Day activity editor in the 400 px inspector put «Сохранить Ctrl+Enter» beside its title, and «Изменить занятие» wrapped to two lines (design review 2 N11). The task pane's footer had already dropped its hints for the same reason (class 159), but through its own context only.
- **Missed because.** The hint follows `useIsWide()` (the window), and the region's room is opt-in (`KeyHintRoomContext`); the activity pane never opted in, and no test rendered a pane header with a keyboard.
- **Class.** Class 159 again: a region-dependent choice made by the window's size, opted into per caller instead of by the region.
- **Sweep and guard.** The activity pane's Save and the composer's form in the pane (`variant="pane"`, «Создать Ctrl+Enter» beside «Новая задача») say there is no room; the shortcuts still work. Other pane headers with a hinted button: none (the task pane's header has no hinted button). Guard: `activity-pane.test.tsx` (wide, with a keyboard: no «Enter» in the pane).

### 183. Bare text placed at the groups' box edge instead of their text inset
- **Bug.** On a computer the task page's description stood at x = 264 under a title at 280, and Settings' «Вы вошли как…», Export / Log out and «Опасные действия» at 264 while every section header's text starts at 280 (16 against 32 on a phone; design review 2 N6, secondary to H1).
- **Missed because.** H1's guard measured the title's x on every screen; nothing compared the loose text under it with the text inset of the groups and headers around it.
- **Class.** A loose element (text, a button with no group around it) given the inset of a *box* (`mx-gutter`, the group's edge) where it lines up with *text* (a row's or a header's text inset).
- **Sweep and guard.** The description (and «Добавить описание») takes the title's inset (`titleInset`); Settings' account text and buttons and the danger zone's heading, openers and notice take `px-row-x` inside the gutter, and the confirm's bordered box keeps the groups' edge. Left at the box edge on purpose: the source text's tinted box and the description editor's field (boxes). Not done: the header actions' right ends (icon glyphs end 12 px inside their 44 targets, so ⋯ at 1164 and «Новый проект» at 1176 while both targets end at 1176), which needs an optical-alignment decision. Guards: `task-screen.test.tsx` (the description's inset is the title's), `settings.test.tsx` (the account's text, Log out and the danger heading at the text inset).

### 184. A server-side bug made fatal to the person's own writes
- **Bug.** Class 170 made `stampSystem` throw on a system event that does not parse, so a dropped revoke could no longer leave its miss standing. But the throw ran inside every push's derive steps (and the alarm's derive through the pool): one derive step that built a bad event would answer every push 500 for as long as the state that makes it lasted, and a client resends its outbox on every sync, so the network would never go quiet and the person's own events would never land (whole-branch review e03360f..1c88671, I2).
- **Missed because.** The guard for 170 tested the happy path (the miss and its revoke in one message); no test made a derive step produce something its schema refuses, because no real state does today.
- **Class.** A failure of the server's own derived work allowed to fail the write that carries the person's data (a consequence that blocks its cause); "fail loudly" taken to mean "throw" where the write must go on.
- **Sweep and guard.** `stampSystem` answers a `Result` for the group; `#system` reports a group that does not parse (`reportUnparsable`, logged under `system/unparsable`) and leaves it out, so each derive step and the sleep pass is skipped alone. `withRetiredMisses` keeps its pair whole: a miss whose revoke does not parse is left out with it, the rest of the batch goes in. Other server-made writes: the parse queue and the pool go through `prepareBatch`, which already answers a `Result`; the alarm's passes are isolated by `runEach`. Guards: `sync.int.test.ts` (a derive step writing an unparsable event: the push answers 200, the person's event lands, a retry writes nothing), `system-events.test.ts` (a miss whose revoke does not parse stays out with it).

### 185. A stored record read, changed and written back by calls that can overlap
- **Bug.** `createSecureEndedSessionStore` read the ended-sessions record, changed one server's list and wrote the whole record back. `retry` removes tokens in parallel (`Promise.all`) and a fresh log-out keeps its token at the same moment: each call wrote what it had read, so the last write dropped the others' changes and a just-kept token could vanish from storage; closed before the server heard the log-out, the session stayed alive (class 140's promise; whole-branch review e03360f..1c88671, M1).
- **Missed because.** Every test awaited one call before the next; none ran two at once against a store whose reads and writes are asynchronous.
- **Class.** An asynchronous read-modify-write of one shared record with no ordering between its callers (a lost update).
- **Sweep and guard.** The queue the poll and calendar already used (`session-poll-queue.ts`'s `seriallyBySession`) is now the shared `serially(key, run)` (`platform/serially.ts`); the ended sessions write under their one key, and the phone's memory (`phone-memory.ts`: dismissed calendar events, the "every time" rules) did the same read-modify-write and now runs it in turn too. Not affected: the drafts (synchronous storage), single-value stores (`session-value.ts`, `shown-then-saved.ts`: one write, no read). Guards: `secure-session.test.ts` (an overlapping keep and removals, two servers at once, a refused write does not stop the next), `phone-memory.test.ts` (two dismissals and two rules at once).

### 186. A client cut that counts a different unit from the server's limit
- **Bug.** `cleanLoginDevice` cut the phone's model, maker, name and system version to the server's limits by grapheme (`clipText`), but the server's zod `.max(n)` counts UTF-16 units: a model of emoji (a family emoji is 8 units) near the limit stayed over it, and the server's `droppedIfInvalid` dropped the field, so the session went unnamed (whole-branch review e03360f..1c88671, M2). The phone's calendar upload cut its texts (title, series, place, notes, link, calendar and organizer names) with `slice`, which keeps to the limit but can split an emoji (class 172's leftover).
- **Missed because.** The login test counted graphemes, so it pinned the wrong unit; no test parsed the client's cut body with the server's own schema.
- **Class.** A length enforced on the client in another unit than the contract counts (graphemes against UTF-16 units), or by code units without a character boundary; class 172's sibling.
- **Sweep and guard.** Device fields and the calendar's texts use core's `sliceWhole` (within the code-unit limit, between two characters). Other client-side cuts of text sent to the server: `fitText` already used `sliceWhole`; the preset slug is ASCII; display cuts (`clipText` in toasts, MCP's `get_task` answer) stay by grapheme on purpose. Guards: `auth.test.ts` (the cut body parses with `endpoints.auth.nonceCreate.body` unchanged; an emoji model keeps 60 units), `phone-calendar.test.ts` (an emoji at the cut of notes and place).

### 187. An agent-facing description left behind by a rule change
- **Bug.** The owner changed how lateness is judged on 2026-11-09 (a task whose subtasks are all done goes by the latest done instant; otherwise by the closing instant), core's `doneLateness` and the close sheet followed, but MCP `close_task` still told agents "done_late is derived when it is past the deadline", so an agent reading it would misreport a task closed after the deadline with every problem solved in time (whole-branch review e03360f..1c88671, M3). `docs/mcp.md` and the spec (`requirements.md`, the per-subtask submission paragraph) carried the old rule too.
- **Missed because.** Tool descriptions are prose no test reads against the rule they describe; the rule's own sweep looked at code paths, not the text that explains them.
- **Class.** A rule restated in prose (a tool description, docs, the spec) that is not part of the rule's change; class 150's documentation face.
- **Sweep and guard.** `close_task`'s description, `docs/mcp.md` and a dated note in the spec now state the rule. Other MCP descriptions that speak of lateness (`list_now`'s "lateness", `rows.ts`'s `(late)` for an open task's deadline) speak of an open task against now and are unaffected. Guard: `mcp-tools.int.test.ts` (close_task's listed description states the rule and not the old sentence).

### 188. A new key in data that older apps parse with a strict schema
- **Bug.** Feedback 5 added `recurrence.firstNumber` to the preset definition. The previous release parses definitions with `z.strictObject` and its preset reducer drops the whole `preset.created` / `preset.updated` when the definition does not parse, so an older app that pulls a preset written with the number loses that event: on a create the preset is missing on that device (its tasks fall back), on an update everything in that update (name, recurrence, deadline policy) is lost there (whole-branch review e03360f..1c88671, I1). The branch had normalised weekday names to numbers for old readers, but no check asked the same of the new key.
- **Missed because.** Compatibility was checked by hand, key by key; nothing parsed what this build writes with the schema the installed apps carry.
- **Class.** Class 13 and 150 in the old-reader direction for data: a key or value added to a shape that is parsed strictly by readers that cannot be updated at once (installed apps), where "unknown key" means "drop the whole event".
- **Sweep and guard.** The owner accepted this one (2026-11-09): update every device before giving a recurrence a first number; the release notes say exactly what an older app loses. Other new keys in stored data on this branch: weekday names (stored as numbers), `submission` (old apps accept it, this build drops it). Guard: `presets/old-reader-compat.test.ts` parses every definition shape this build stores with the previous release's schema, frozen in `presets/previous-release/preset-schema.e03360f.frozen.ts`, pins `firstNumber` as the only shape old apps refuse, and fails when the current schema gains a key the test does not classify. Replace the frozen copy with each release's own when it ships.

### 189. A centred bubble wider than its trigger, placed by sides only
- **Bug.** On Now at 1280 the running card's Stop sits at the card's right edge, «Чем заняты?» 41 px to its right, «Да, ещё» before it and the alongside row's Stop under it. Centred above, «Стоп: Пошёл в ЦСС» (141 px over a 44 px trigger) lay 7 px over the activity field; every other side covered a button too, so the placement fell back to the first side and covered the field (`overlays.e2e.ts`, after design N1/N3 put the tonal Stop there).
- **Missed because.** `tooltip-place.test.ts` placed bubbles beside one obstacle at a time; nothing put a trigger in a crowd where every side is taken centred but a slide along a side is clear.
- **Class.** A placement that tries only the four centred positions and then gives up, where the bubble is wider than its trigger and can slide along a side while still lying over it (floating-ui's "shift").
- **Sweep and guard.** `placeTooltip` tries every side centred first (the old order, so nothing that fitted moves), then the same sides lined up with the trigger's end, then its start; the fallback stays the first centred place. Other overlays: `placePopover` belongs to a modal popover with its own dim (covering the page is its job). Guard: `tooltip-place.test.ts` (the running card's crowd: above, slid, over no control, still over its trigger); `overlays.e2e.ts` sweeps Now, Day and a task.

### 190. A dismissal put in by an effect after a show, missing the press that comes in the same moment
- **Bug.** In the full e2e run, on a phone-width window, Escape left the time bar's «Calendar» sheet open (`sheet-focus.e2e.ts`, `time.e2e.ts`: the next tap landed on the sheet). The Calendar tile has an immediate tooltip («nothing on in your calendar»): a click's enter showed it at once, and its press came before React ran the effect that listens for presses on the window (243ac04's «a press hides any tooltip»). The tooltip stayed, under the sheet the press opened, registered as Escape's first taker, so Escape took only the tooltip. A delayed tooltip did the same when its trigger was pressed within the 400 ms: the waiting show was never called off and came up under the sheet.
- **Missed because.** The unit test for «a press hides it» fired the press after the show had rendered and its effects had run; alone the e2e run won the race (Chrome's boundary events after the sheet came up usually sent a leave first), under load it lost.
- **Class.** A listener that must hear the very next event, installed in a passive effect after the state that needs it (one render later than the event can come); a pending timer that a user's action should cancel left running.
- **Sweep and guard.** The tooltip's own wrapper hides on `pointerdown` (React's handler, there from the first render), which also cancels a waiting show. Others: the Escape taker also comes in by effect, but an Escape in that gap closes the sheet and the tooltip goes with its trigger (no harm); popovers and sheets are opened by the press itself. Guards: `tooltip.web.test.tsx` (enter and press in one go with the window's listener not yet in: nothing stays; a press during the delay: nothing comes).

### 191. A narrowed column that left two buttons side by side with labels that no longer fit
- **Bug.** Offline, Settings' Log out confirm at 390 drew «Wait» and «Sign out anyway» 60 px high (`sync-state.e2e.ts` wants one line, 44–56): class 183's fix moved the account's actions to the text inset, 32 px narrower, and each half of the side-by-side row became 159 px, too narrow for «Sign out anyway» / «Всё равно выйти» at the button's 20 px padding.
- **Missed because.** The N6 change was checked by the inset each block starts at (jest) and by shots; the confirm only appears offline with a change waiting, and only the e2e opens it at 390.
- **Class.** Class 176's sibling: a layout change narrows a container, and a row of controls inside it keeps its side-by-side split, so the labels wrap; the danger zone's confirm already stacked on a phone for the same reason (design review H3).
- **Sweep and guard.** On a phone the Log out confirm stacks Wait over Sign out anyway, each the column's width, as the danger zone's `SheetActions isStacked`. Other side-by-side pairs under the text inset: the danger openers stack on a phone; Export and Log out stack on a phone. Guard: `settings.test.tsx` (on a phone no row between the confirm's group and its buttons); `sync-state.e2e.ts` (one line each at 390).

### 192. A test that leaves the page while a fire-and-forget request is still in flight
- **Bug.** In the full e2e run the assistant log showed «Suggested a new task “разобрать почту”» with «A task … was created after this» where `assistant.e2e.ts` wanted «Created task …» and «You approved it». The approval's answer to the log (`assistant.resolve`, best effort and not awaited, behind a CORS preflight) was cancelled by the test's `page.goto("/")` right after the press; under load the preflight alone outlasted it. A probe that held `/api/decisions/resolve` for 1.5 s drew the failing row every time; the Worker's log had preflights for it with no POST after them.
- **Missed because.** The test waited for what the screen shows (the line emptied, the task in Now), which comes from the local store and the outbox, not for the log's own request; alone the request usually won.
- **Class.** A test's next step (a reload, a navigation) races a request the app sends without awaiting it; the steady state was asserted by a proxy (the screen) that does not depend on that request.
- **Sweep and guard.** Both journeys that read the log after an approval wait for the `POST /api/decisions/resolve` answer (`approveAndLog`). Other un-awaited sends read back by a test: none in e2e (the other approvals do not read the log). The product keeps it best effort (its doc says a lost answer leaves the row undecided). **Done 2026-11-09 (owner-approved):** the answer now goes with `keepalive: true` (`CallOptions.keepalive` in `@pace/client`'s API client; the body is a few ids, far under the 64 KB cap; Chromium keeps a keepalive request through its CORS preflight), so a reload or a closed tab right after Approve no longer drops it. Guards: `api-client.test.ts`, `create-client.test.ts` (the resolve call, and only it, carries keepalive), `assistant.e2e.ts` (the answer held 1.5 s past a `page.goto` still lands in the log).

### 193. A one-shot read of a layout that is drawn before it settles
- **Bug.** `week.e2e.ts` (the narrow «Email the dean» box is cut with an ellipsis) failed in the full run and in one of two runs of its file, and passed alone 12 of 12: its `evaluate` ran once, right after the box became visible. The failure screenshot, taken a moment later, shows the box cut («Email t…»); a frame-by-frame probe showed the box first drawn 19 px wide, then 60 once measured. The Week grid settles in steps (the body's height, then the hours' share of it; the box's measured width, then its label's fit), so under load one read can land between them.
- **Missed because.** Locally the steps finish before the read; nothing in the test waited for the settled state, only for the box to exist.
- **Class.** A geometry assertion made once, on the first frame the element exists, about something the app arrives at over several frames, instead of polled until the layout holds (class 60's kind on the test's side: a decision on a snapshot that is about to change).
- **Sweep and guard.** The check is an `expect.poll` with the same predicate (the box still has to end up cut). Other one-shot geometry reads in the Week spec run after an action whose result they wait for (a hover's bubble, a picked box's details); the layout sweeps in `geometry.ts` already settle first. Root cause of the uncut moment not pinned: the probes never caught an uncut frame.

### 194. A container's key left unwired because a field inside it could not yet claim the key first
- **Bug.** On Now at a computer's width Escape did nothing to the task pane: `now-screen.tsx` gave the kit's `Shell` `inspectorOpen` but no `onInspectorClose`, so `InspectorFrame`'s Escape was never armed (QA round 24 R24-01). It had been left out on purpose (class 155): the pane's in-place description editor did not own Escape, so the pane's Escape would have closed the pane under a half-typed edit.
- **Missed because.** Class 155 wrote the gap down instead of closing it, and its guards checked the panes that were wired (Inbox, Week, Day); nothing listed the shell's callers that pass `inspectorOpen` without `onInspectorClose`.
- **Class.** A container behaviour held back because a child could not take the key first, where the fix is to give the child the key (the registry's `data-pace-keys="escape"`, `escape-first.ts`) and then wire the container, not to leave the container without it.
- **Sweep and guard.** The description editor (`task-description.tsx`) takes `escapeFieldProps` (`platform/enter-key`) and cancels its edit on Escape; the registry leaves that Escape alone and its keyup is held back, so the next Escape closes the pane. The same editor shows in the Inbox's review pane, which already closed on Escape: it no longer drops an edit there. Other panes' fields: Day's editor has its own layer; Week and Inbox have no field in place. The task form in Now's pane keeps its own Escape (its close), so Now wires `onInspectorClose` only while a task is picked. Guards: `now-screen.test.tsx` (Escape closes; Escape in the editor cancels only), `keyboard.e2e.ts` (Escape closes, focus back on the row; an edit takes the first Escape, the second closes).

### 195. A request met for the reload and the shared link, and written off for history, because the library's default did not do it
- **Bug.** Feedback 5 item 6 asked that Back and forward follow the screen; opening a task beside Now's list, another day, week or period, or a filter replaced the browser entry, so Back left the app (QA round 24 R24-02). The front report listed it as a deviation: expo-router's tab navigator keeps its history length on a param change, so its linking replaces the address.
- **Missed because.** The tests checked what the address holds (`router.setParams` called, the param read back after a reload), not how many steps the history has; "the library has no push-params" closed the question without looking at what the library does expose (a router override, the history its linking counts).
- **Class.** A user-facing requirement downgraded to a documented deviation at the first library limit, without a test that states the requirement itself (Back after a change returns to what was there), so nothing kept it open.
- **Sweep and guard.** `useSearchParam` takes a step before every change it writes (one per action, none for the value already shown) through `PUSH_ENTRY`, which the tabs' router override (`ui/tab-history.ts`) turns into one more tab-history entry; expo-router then pushes. Every param of feedback 5 item 6 goes through the hook (Now's task and project, Day's date, Week's week and block, Insights' week, History's moment, the Inbox's open item). The other deviations of that report were re-read: they are product choices (narrow windows open `/task/<id>`, the editor and the composer form not in the address), not library limits. Guards: `tab-history.test.ts`, `use-search-param.test.tsx`, `reload-state.e2e.ts` (Back and forward on Now's pane, Day, Week).

### 196. One instant's day named two ways on one screen
- **Bug.** In the task pane a subtask's done mark read «готово пн 22:08» for a mark made today, while the task's fields beside it say «сегодня» (QA round 24 R24-07). The mark built its own "weekday clock" label with `Intl` instead of the formatter the fields use (`zonedText`, `formatRelativeDay`).
- **Missed because.** The test asserted the mark's text as the code built it ("Wed 12:50"), not against how the same screen names that day elsewhere.
- **Class.** A date label formatted locally (a fresh `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `weekday`) where the app has one relative-day formatter, so the same day reads "today" in one place and a weekday in another.
- **Sweep and guard.** The done mark uses `zonedText(… "due" …)` on the device's zone ("today 22:08"). Swept `weekday: "short"` labels of an instant: the notifications digest named a deadline "Fri 18:00" while the task says "tomorrow 18:00"; it now uses `formatRelativeDay`. The others name a calendar day in a calendar (Day's header, Week's strip and boxes, the date picker, a recurrence's weekdays), where the weekday is the point. Guards: `task-screen.test.tsx` ("done today 12:50"), `notifications-screen.test.tsx` ("tomorrow 18:00 · …").

### 197. A submit that is only faded when something is missing, so a press or its shortcut says nothing
- **Bug.** New task with an empty title: Create was disabled (faded) and Ctrl+Enter or Enter did nothing, with no word on what was missing (QA round 24 R24-06, R22-15). Projects' and Presets' forms say «Give the project a name.» on the field in the same case.
- **Missed because.** The composer's tests asserted the disabled button as the expected state of an empty form; the touched gate (QA round 14 L6: a fresh form does not blame the title) was read as "never say it before typing", and the keyboard path had no test with an empty title.
- **Class.** A form's required field enforced by disabling the submit, where the kit's pattern is a submit that stays live and, once tried, says on the field what is missing (the `isSubmitted` gate of `TextField`) and puts the cursor there.
- **Sweep and guard.** The composer's form counts its tries (`submitTries` to `TaskFields`): Create stays live while only the title is missing; a try shows «Give the task a title.» on the title and focuses it; the fresh form still does not blame it. Other forms: Projects and Presets already refuse on the field; the task editor's Save is disabled only after the title was emptied there, which already shows the error (touched); the server sheet's Check is a lookup, not a form with a field error. Guards: `composer.test.tsx` (Create on an empty form asks for the title and makes nothing), `composer.e2e.ts` (Alt+N, Ctrl+Enter and Create say it, focus in the title).

### 198. A field that focuses itself as an overlay opens, noted by the modal as the place to give focus back to
- **Bug.** On Now at a computer's width, Escape on the time bar's Task picker left focus on the page instead of the «Task» tile (QA round 24 R24-05). Its search field had `autoFocus`: it took focus as it mounted, before react-native-web's modal ran the effect that notes the element to give focus back to, so the modal noted the field (gone after the close). The full run also showed the Calendar picker's tile losing focus the same way.
- **Missed because.** The pickers' tests checked what opens and what a pick does; only Sheets had an e2e for focus going back, and the popover's comment ("focus goes back to the trigger") relied on the modal without a test.
- **Class.** Class 5's kind (focus left on the page when a control goes away) through an ordering trap: a child focusing itself in mount, ahead of the container's own record of the opener.
- **Sweep and guard.** The Task search has no `autoFocus`; the popover's `useSheetFocusRef` already puts the keyboard on its first field once open (only with a keyboard, fix round 5 V4). `Popover` now gives focus back to its anchor a frame after it goes, where nothing else took it (`useFocusBack`). Other `autoFocus` fields: the subtask entry opens on a press inside an already open form, the project and preset names and the description editor are on the page, not in an overlay; `add-computer.tsx` already avoids it for this reason. Guard: `sheet-focus.e2e.ts` (each of the four pickers: Escape gives focus back to its tile; the Task search has the keyboard while open).

### 199. A derived copy of user content shown beside the content once the content itself became rich
- **Bug.** A task whose description holds an address showed it twice in the pane: a header link «example.com/a ↗» above the facts (the view-model's `link` falls back to the first address in the source text or the description) and the same address as a link inside the description (QA round 24 R24-08).
- **Missed because.** The header link predates clickable descriptions (feedback 5 item 8); when the description learnt to show its links, nothing re-read the surfaces that showed a link taken from it.
- **Class.** A fallback that surfaces part of a field elsewhere, kept after the field started showing that part itself.
- **Sweep and guard.** The header link is left out when the description shows that very address (`isInDescription`, by the same `splitLinks` the description draws with); an explicit link, or one from the source text only, still shows. The edit form's Link field keeps its fallback (a field, not a second display). Guard: `task-screen.test.tsx` (an address in the description is one link on the screen).

### 200. One glyph for two meanings in one row
- **Bug.** Each row of Settings → Presets drew three chevrons: › (open the preset) inside the row and ▲ ▼ (move it up or down) beside it, the same glyph family at the same size, so the row read as three of one control (QA round 24 R24-10).
- **Missed because.** The reorder buttons were tested by name and state; nothing looked at which glyphs a row draws.
- **Class.** A row whose separate actions share one icon family (the disclosure chevron), so the one that navigates is not told from the ones that act.
- **Sweep and guard.** The move buttons draw `arrow-up` / `arrow-down` (ArrowDown added to the kit); the row keeps its one chevron. Other chevrons: Day's activity field and the task header's project use `chevron-down` as the one picker cue of their control; the composer form's fold is `chevron-up` alone in its header. Guard: `presets.test.tsx` (the move buttons draw no chevron; the row's link draws the one).

### 201. Local state that belongs to a param, kept when the param changes without the screen's own setter
- **Bug.** After browser Back changed Day's date or Week's week, the block open in Day's inspector or sheet, or the calendar event picked on Week, still showed the day or week left behind (review of the round 24 fixes, I1). `onDate` / `onWeek` cleared the selection only when the change came from inside the app; Back calls `resetRoot`, keeps the screen mounted and changes only the param.
- **Missed because.** Before R24-02 Back left the screen, so a param change always came through the screen's own setter, and the tests changed the date only through its buttons. Nothing changed the address from outside while a selection was open.
- **Class.** React state that is only meaningful for one value of an address param (a selection on a date, a week, a list), reset in the setter's handler instead of being tied to the value it belongs to.
- **Sweep and guard.** Day keeps `{ date, target }` and Week `{ weekStart, picked }`; a mismatch reads as no selection, derived and never written back (class 1). Swept every screen that reads a param: History (the confirm state lives per row), Insights (no local state), the Inbox (the open item is the param itself), Now (the composer form takes precedence over the pick by design). `use-search-param.ts` says it at the hook: local state for one value of the param stores that value with it. Guards: `day-screen.test.tsx`, `day-wide.test.tsx`, `week-wide.test.tsx` (the address changed from outside drops the pick), `reload-state.e2e.ts` (a block open on yesterday, Back: no pane).

### 202. A history step left behind by an action that removed what the step opened
- **Bug.** Opening an Inbox note takes a history step (`?open=<id>`); accepting it closed the details through the pane's close, which took one more step. Triaging N notes left 2N entries that all showed the same list, and deleting a task in Now's pane did the same with `?task=<deleted>` (review of the round 24 fixes, I2).
- **Missed because.** The R24-02 tests counted the steps of opening and closing, not of an action that removes the item; the pane's "done" reused its "close", so the removal took the step a person's close takes.
- **Class.** A removal routed through a navigation that records a step (close, back), so history keeps an entry whose subject is gone.
- **Sweep and guard.** `useSearchParam` gives a third function, `forget`: on the web it takes back the step that opened the item when the entry before is the same screen without the param (`isStepBackWithout`, `useStepBack`), else it takes the param off in place; never a new step. The Inbox's details (Accept, Delete, a decision answered) and its rows (on the note that is open) call it; the task pane's Delete leaves through `useTaskGoneBack` (`TaskPaneProvider`'s `onGone`). Swept: Day's and Week's selections are not in the address except Week's block, which cannot be deleted from Week; History's moment and the filters name nothing that an action removes; a project filter whose project is archived falls back to all without writing (class 1). Left as is: an older entry that named the item before it was reopened (an entry two steps back) still names it and shows the list. Guards: `tab-history.test.ts`, `use-search-param.test.tsx`, `inbox-wide.test.tsx`, `now-screen.test.tsx` (no step on removal), `reload-state.e2e.ts` (Back after accept and after delete goes to where the screen was opened from).

### 203. One form's rule kept to the entry path it was found on
- **Bug.** Day's gap «Записать» and «Записать прошедшее» saved a block that ran into the next activity, and the timeline cut the running «Собираюсь» from 1 h to 22 min without a word; the block's own edit form refuses the same overlap («Это заходит на следующую активность…») (QA round 25 R25-01).
- **Missed because.** `nextStartOf` answered only for an edit target (`kind: "edit"`) and left logged blocks out, so the check that came from an edit-form bug never ran on the log paths; its test drove the edit form only.
- **Class.** Class 2 (entry paths): a validation written for the form where the bug was seen, while the other ways into the same data write without it.
- **Sweep and guard.** `nextStartOf` takes the draft's start and every main activity, live or logged, except the block itself, for every target: a gap's Log, Log past and the edit form (sheet and inspector share `useActivityEditor`), so a block may not run into the next one whichever of them wrote it. Week's block view is read-only. Left as the model has it: the agent's `log_activity` (MCP) still lets a logged block win over the live time it covers, which is the tool's job (retroactive corrections). Guards: `day-screen.test.tsx` (the refusal from a gap's Log, Log past and a block's form into a logged block), `day-wide.test.tsx` (the inspector).

### 204. A soft delete whose way back lives only in another screen
- **Bug.** Deleting a preset gave no toast and no Undo, a deleted preset opened as an ordinary editable form (saving kept it deleted), its only way back was History's «Вернуть» on «Пресет архивирован», and with «Показать удалённые» on the deleted rows sat among the live ones with working ▲▼ (QA round 25 R25-02).
- **Missed because.** The preset tests checked that Delete archived the preset; nothing opened a deleted one or compared the delete with the app's other deletes (Inbox note, task: a toast with Undo).
- **Class.** A soft delete (archive) built as a flag the screens do not read: the item stays editable and orderable as if live, and the undo convention of the other deletes is not followed.
- **Sweep and guard.** `restorePreset` revokes the newest effective `preset.archived` (a correction; the log keeps both). Delete shows «Пресет удалён: …» with Undo; a deleted preset opens read-only with «Удалён», what it means and «Восстановить пресет»; deleted rows sort after the live ones, without ▲▼, and the New-from chips offer live presets only. Other soft deletes: projects restore from their own screen (`project.restore`), an Inbox note and a task have the toast's Undo, a series' stop is in History by design (a correction of a schedule). Guards: `actions.test.ts` (restore revokes, twice), `presets.test.tsx` (Undo after Delete; the deleted preset read-only with Restore; deleted rows last without arrows).
